## Snapshot
SanDisk is a pure-play **NAND flash** maker and storage-solutions vendor — flash components plus SSDs, memory cards and drives sold under the SanDisk and WD brands (FY25 10-K, 2025-08-21). It is the **standalone flash business spun out of Western Digital**, which separated on **Feb 21, 2025**; trades on Nasdaq as SNDK with only a short post-spin filing history (one 10-K, six 10-Qs as of Jun-2026). It runs a **single reportable segment** but reports three end-markets: **Data Center, Edge (PC/mobile), and Consumer** (10-K; Q3 FY26 transcript, 2026-04-30). Bits come from the long-standing **Kioxia "Flash Ventures" JV** (BiCS NAND fabs in Yokkaichi & Kitakami, Japan; JV extended to Dec-2034). It competes with Samsung, SK Hynix and YMTC in flash (FY25 10-K). The thesis: NAND is being repriced from a commodity into a strategic AI-storage input — enterprise/QLC SSD for the data-center capacity tier and KV-cache, plus on-device-AI premium storage at the edge. The demand math that underpinned the re-rate: MS sizes an **incremental ~US$29bn AI-NAND TAM by 2029**, with AI NAND reaching **34% of the global NAND market / 431EB (~20% of bit shipments) by 2029**, driven by CSP AI-inference storage (MS AI NAND overview, 2025-09-10).

## At a glance — product · buyer · supplier
| | |
|---|---|
| **Sells (top 3)** | 1) Enterprise/QLC SSD (data center) · 2) Raw NAND flash components · 3) Client/consumer SSDs, memory cards & drives (SanDisk/WD) |
| **Main buyer(s)** | Hyperscalers / data-center & enterprise (largest growth), client OEMs, consumer channel — top-10 customers a large share of revenue |
| **Key suppliers** | Kioxia "Flash Ventures" JV (BiCS NAND bits, Yokkaichi/Kitakami) + wafer/equipment; Nanya (DRAM, $1B) |

## Position in the value chain
SanDisk sits in the **middle** of the flash chain: it co-develops and co-manufactures BiCS NAND with **Kioxia** (Flash Ventures JV) plus buys equipment/wafers, turns it into flash + SSD/storage solutions, and sells to **datacenter/enterprise (hyperscalers), client OEMs and consumer** channels. Short post-spin history (standalone since Feb-2025) means limited standalone comparables — model off the WDC-era flash segment plus six quarters of SNDK filings. In NAND, **QLC eSSD** is the structural growth vector: it is "the most optimal storage solution" for the randomized-I/O needs of AI training/inference (pre-trained models, checkpoints, KV-cache off-loading), displacing both TLC NAND and nearline HDD (MS AI NAND overview, 2025-09-10). SNDK still trails Micron and Hynix/Solidigm in eSSD share; the **BiCS 8** transition + controller investment is management's path to close that gap.

## Current state (latest quarter)

**🆕🔴 2026-08-27 — THE JV PARTNER SIDE OF A ~¥5 TRILLION (>$31bn) JAPANESE NAND CAPEX PROGRAMME THROUGH FY2031: SANDISK AND [[KIOXIA]] COMMIT JOINTLY TO A NEW K3 FAB AT KITAKAMI (FY2029 START) PLUS Y7 ADDITIONS.** (JPM · **Mio Shikanai / Jay Kwon** — the note covers KIOXIA (OW, PT ¥130,000); **SanDisk is Harlan Sur's name at JPM and this note carries NO SNDK rating, PT or estimate change.** Company announcement 17:50 JST, **2026-08-27**.)
- **WHAT IS ACTUALLY COMMITTED: ~¥5trn of NAND FRONT-END PRODUCTION EQUIPMENT in Japan across FY2026-31, made jointly by the two JV partners and *"on the assumption of support from the Japanese government."*** Of it, **~¥1.8trn is the third Kitakami fab (K3), building plus equipment, with operations starting FY2029**; the rest is added equipment at **Y7 (Yokkaichi)** plus areas not yet disclosed.
- ⚠️⚠️ **THE SPLIT IS NOT 50/50 AND IT IS GOVERNMENT-CONTINGENT — THIS IS THE ONE THING NOT TO GET WRONG: *"Each company's actual amount could change depending on the scale of support. **WITHOUT ANY SUPPORT, Kioxia's amount would be 60% of the ¥5 trillion total, or about ¥3 trillion.**"*** ➜ **Which implies SanDisk's unsupported share is the residual ~40% (~¥2trn, ~$12-13bn) over six years — an INFERENCE FROM KIOXIA'S DISCLOSED SHARE, not a SanDisk disclosure. No SNDK capex guidance was given in this announcement and none should be carried.**
- 🔴 **THE DEMAND ANCHOR IS UNCHANGED, AND THAT IS THE POINT: Kioxia confirmed **no change to the NAND bit-growth forecast from its June Investor Day (22% CAGR CY2025-28E)** and said the partners *"plan to continue investing **in line with the market's growth**."*** ➜ **Read against this page's own supply debate: MS cut 2027 industry NAND bit-supply growth to 26% (from 31%) on 08-25, while TrendForce warned of **oversupply by late 2027** off the YMTC raise (WSJ, 08-24). **The K3 capacity lands FY2029 — after that window.** It does not resolve the 2027 argument in either direction and is not netted against it.**
- **JPM's framing of the strategic logic, which applies to both partners equally: *"management's existing view that **current capacity can meet demand through EARLY 2029** but that **greenfield investment will be needed thereafter**."*** ➜ **This dates the greenfield cliff for the whole JV — i.e. for the capacity base that carries SanDisk's bit supply too.**
- ⚠️ **AND THE ONE ITEM WITH NEAR-TERM P&L RELEVANCE: the FY2026-28 spending envelope (¥1.4trn at the Kioxia level) is UNCHANGED and already includes part of K3 and the Y7 adds — but *"management said it is **FRONT-LOADING the investment somewhat to meet strong demand**."* Front-loading inside an unchanged envelope is a cash-timing statement, not a capacity statement.**
- ⚠️ **OPEN AND UNATTRIBUTED: JPM flags that beyond K3 and Y7 there is no disclosure of what the remaining ~¥3.2trn of the headline is for, nor the specific K3/Y7 timelines. Also open: *"targeted for Japan for now, but management appears to be leaving room to consider sites in Japan AND OVERSEAS."***


**🆕🔴 2026-08-27 — JEFFERIES ANNUAL SEMICONDUCTOR CONFERENCE (~100 companies), THE PRIMARY NOTE.** ⚠️ **This page already carries the conference’s top-line messaging from a desk relay; what follows is the COMPANY-SPECIFIC block from the FULL note, which the relay did not carry.** House framing: *"the DISCONNECT between the TOUGH TAPE and POSITIVE UNDERLYING FUNDAMENTALS was front and center… messaging from management teams throughout Compute, Analog, Semi-Cap, Optical, Memory and everything in between was ALMOST UNIFORMLY POSITIVE"*, with the market *"shifted away from fundamentals towards a GLASS-HALF-EMPTY view of AI focused on frontier-lab economics, open-source model competition, concerns of CIRCULAR FINANCING, and POLITICAL PUSHBACK on AI DCs."* (**Jefferies · Blayne Curtis, Janardan Menon, Kevin Garrigan et al.**, "2026 Semiconductor Conference Recap", 2026-08-27) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_Semiconductor_Conference_Recap.html)
- 🔴 **SUPPLY DISCIPLINE IS THE WHOLE THESIS AND IT IS NOW DATED THROUGH C29: *"**Supply likely remains DISCIPLINED THROUGH C29**, with **YMTC the main BIT-GROWTH OUTLIER**."*** ➤ **One named risk to the discipline argument, and it is Chinese. That is the falsifiable part — and [[ASML]] separately told the same conference that YMTC cleanrooms come online through 2027, which is the mechanism by which that outlier would arrive.**
- 🔴 **THE NBM COVERAGE NUMBER IS THE MODEL-RELEVANT ONE: *"**NBMs cover ~67% of F28 BITS** and could HYPOTHETICALLY reach **80-90% if PC and MOBILE customers also enter into agreements**."* Mechanics: *"**NBM commitments require an INVENTORY BUILD in F27**, while pricing includes **FIXED AND VARIABLE structures with FLOORS AND CEILINGS**."*
- ⚠️⚠️ **FLOORS *AND* CEILINGS — THE CONTRACTS CUT OFF THE UPSIDE AS WELL AS THE DOWNSIDE. Anyone modelling SNDK to spot NAND pricing in a shortage is modelling the wrong curve for two-thirds of F28 bits. This is the single most important qualification on this page for the memory-upcycle thesis, and it also means the F27 inventory build is a WORKING-CAPITAL cost incurred to secure that coverage.**
- 🔴 **HBF, WITH A TRADE RATIO: *"**HBF sampling begins in C27 and uses SLC NAND, implying a 3-4x TRADE RATIO versus TLC/QLC**."* ➤ Every HBF bit consumes 3-4x the wafer of a mainstream bit, so HBF adoption is itself a SUPPLY-TIGHTENING event — the product and the pricing thesis reinforce each other.**
- **STRATEGY, STATED PLAINLY: *"focus remains on **SUSTAINABLE MARGINS rather than MAXIMISING BIT GROWTH**, with any shift toward SLC **FURTHER TIGHTENING supply**."*

🔴 **WHAT IS STILL LIVE IN IT — ①  THE HBF SPECIFICATION, WHICH IS THE MOST COMPLETE ONE ON THIS PAGE AND IT IS SOURCED TO SANDISK'S OWN ANALYST DAY: *"At its 2025 Analyst Day, SNDK noted a move into **HBF (High Bandwidth Flash)**, driving improved **1.6-2.3 Tbps bandwidth (~60x higher than PCIe6.0 eSSD's 28Gbps and >5,000x vs HDD's ~300Mbps)** WHILE delivering **10x NAND capacity at 4TB/8TB vs 288-500GB HBM/GPU**. We believe HBF could increase NAND penetration into the AI server with **FIRST SAMPLES OF HBF POTENTIALLY IN 2H26E, WITH PRODUCTION IN 2027E**."*** 🔴 **AND THE SUPPLY-SIDE TWIST, WHICH IS THE NON-OBVIOUS PART AND THE REASON THIS NOTE IS WORTH KEEPING: *"We believe HBF launches could lead to **HIGHER NAND SUPPLY CONSTRAINTS, as HBF stacks 16 LAYERS OF SLC-NAND, creating some of the same 'TRADE RATIO' CONSTRAINTS in conventional NAND supply.**"*** ➜ **That is the exact mechanism [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md) already carries for HBM (Micron's "HBM3E is ~3x the silicon of DDR5 for the same bits, and the ratio is getting worse") applied to NAND: HBF is bit-destructive to conventional supply. **A product that consumes 16 SLC layers per stack is a NAND-tightness driver, not just a new revenue line — and that cuts for SNDK's pricing and against everyone who buys conventional NAND.** ⚠️ **"First samples potentially 2H26E, production 2027E" is now testable — 2H26 is the current window. Check it at the next SNDK print.** ⚠️ **The Mizuho headline framing ("6x capacity of HBM, 100x bandwidth of TLC SSD") is INTERNALLY INCONSISTENT with the body's own "10x NAND capacity at 4TB/8TB vs 288-500GB HBM" and "~60x vs PCIe6.0 eSSD" — the title and the exhibit do not agree. Both are recorded; neither is adopted as the number.**
🔴 **② THE KV-CACHE DEMAND MECHANISM, QUANTIFIED: *"We estimate that an **AI INFERENCE-OPTIMIZED RACK could have >4x NAND vs. a similar TRAINING-ONLY AI rack**, leading to upside with a higher mix shift towards inference workloads as agentic AI ramps."* Supporting spec: *"TLC/QLC eSSD read/write speeds are **>100x vs. HDDs**, with leading **QLC eSSD capacities now reaching ~123TB and leading HDDs at ~32TB**. Key hyperscale focus is on expanding KVCache, with **16TB/GPU NAND** and potential HBF, driving inference economics… for better GPU utilization."*** ➜ **A 4x NAND content step from training to inference racks is the cleanest single argument on this page for why the NAND cycle is not the same cycle as the 2021 one, and it is independent of price. Read it against the 2026-08-26 [[NVDA]] print, where management guided FQ3 sequential growth to come primarily from the ACIE/inference half of data centre — the mix is moving toward the 4x-content racks.**
⚠️ **③ THE SUPPLY BACKDROP AS OF APRIL, recorded as a dated baseline: *"We estimate **NAND supply wafer capacity down ~6% y/y in 2026E**, with 2027E flattish as focus remains on node transitions to drive bits… We estimate **NAND contract pricing up 81%/20% for 2Q-3Q26E** with potential for further upside on supply tightness."*** ⚠️⚠️ **THE +81%/+20% q/q CONTRACT-PRICING PATH IS AN APRIL FORECAST FOR QUARTERS THAT HAVE NOW LARGELY PASSED. It is NOT carried as a current estimate and must not be blended with the spot/contract marks this wiki tracks live — but it is a usable scorecard: if realised NAND contract pricing came in near +81% in 2Q26, Mizuho's supply framework was right and its later, higher targets have a track record behind them. **That check is not performed here and is flagged as an open item.** *"The transition to 375L/459L NAND is the key driver of higher bit shipments in 2026/27E."* [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/The_DC_Storage_Evolution_-_Upside_KVCache-QLC_eSSDs_to_HBF.html)

Q4 tracking +5-10% q/q** — which for all your rate-of-change lovers is **low triple digits (~200%) y/y vs 700%+ y/y in Q3**"* (TMTB EOD Wrap, 2026-08-25). ⚠️ **Buy-side desk channel figure, no source named, blended across DRAM and NAND — but it is the first *sequential* mark on this page that puts a number on the deceleration while keeping the level rising.** 🔴 **The y/y comparison is the point: prices are still going UP, but the second derivative turns hard negative between Q3 and Q4. Any model that extrapolates the Q3 y/y print into 2027 is extrapolating the comp, not the price.**

**Corroborating from the supply side the same day (BofA / Simon Woo relaying [[SAMSUNG]] IR, 2026-08-25):** **DRAM up 20%+ QoQ in 3Q, then high-single-digit% in 4Q, then "broadly stable through the quarters of 2027"** — with **DRAM OP margin ~80%+/-** and **memory ASP "negotiable on a quarterly basis EVEN UNDER LTAs"** (LTAs target 60-70% of wafer capacity). **JPM's Asia Tech Tour reads the same set-up as tighter for longer: HBM ASP +43% y/y in 27E, blended server memory content +28% y/y in 27E, both with upside risk** (JPM · Jay Kwon, 2026-08-25).

**🆕🔴 2026-08-24 — SUSQUEHANNA NAMES SANDISK ONE OF ITS TWO PREFERRED MEMORY NAMES, AND THE THESIS IS A SPECIFIC PRODUCT INFLECTION RATHER THAN A PRICE CALL: "SanDisk, where we see an inflection point in the adoption of 2Tb QLC-based NAND and eSSD solutions."** (SIG · Mehdi Hosseini w/ Bastien Faucon-Morin, "Technology Hardware — Memory Update: Intra-Quarter Pricing Update", 2026-08-24 — **Positive, px $1,596.08**; the other preferred name is [[MU]] on HBM share.)
- 🔴 **The NAND pricing marks are running well ahead of SIG's own published estimates, and the eSSD line is the standout:** **3Q26 NAND contract prices tracking +60% q/q on average versus the ~35% q/q embedded in SIG's published 3Q26 estimates** (2Q26 actual blended NAND ASP was **+65% q/q**); **blended contract eSSD ASPs tracking ~+70% q/q, "significantly outpacing price gains reflected in the spot market"**; and **4Q26 blended NAND ASPs forecast at ≥+25% q/q, "exceeding the assumptions currently embedded in our published estimates."** *"Enterprise demand, in particular, appears to be strengthening."*
- **Corroboration from the JPM tour on the demand mechanism rather than the price:** memory suppliers described **ongoing industry initiatives to use eSSD for KV-cache offloading** as HBM/DRAM cost inflates the BOM, with **TLC NAND viewed as the primary solution** — plus emerging inference-oriented alternatives (**SLC NAND, super-high-IOPS, Z-NAND**, the latter initially targeting edge AI) that *"could add to the supply tightness."* (JPM · Jay Kwon, 2026-08-23)
- **The hyperscaler adoption story, from Hot Chips:** **META tested placing lower-cost QLC SSDs between TLC SSDs and hard drives and found it improved performance, prompting broader hyperscaler SSD adoption.** Simultaneously AI infrastructure pushed HDD demand into shortage, so **QLC SSDs began filling both the new tier and, in some cases, replacing unavailable hard drives at much higher cost — "that helped push NAND into shortage as well, benefiting [[KIOXIA]] and SNDK."** (Jim Handy, Objective Analysis, at Hot Chips 2026, via TMT Breakout, 2026-08-23 — **conference coverage, not broker research.**)
- ⚠️ **The supply-side caveat that cuts the other way, and it is the bear case for 2027:** at the JPM tour *"investors expressed greater concerns on NAND"* than DRAM, *"turning conservative on 2027 S-D citing YMTC capacity expansion and consumer NAND degradation."* JPM's own resolution is that **eSSD demand will be strong enough to fully offset consumer NAND weakness** — but that is JPM's view, not the suppliers'. Supply timing is the offsetting comfort: **new Korean NAND cleanroom space no earlier than 2029E**, with Kioxia likewise relying on **BiCS 9/10 migration** rather than capacity. (JPM · Kwon, 2026-08-23)
- **HBF, which this page has tracked since FMS, is being talked down by the suppliers themselves:** *"we sensed a relative conservatism citing the lack of interest from customers and a clear value proposition… High cost structure (TSV processing and system level upgrade) was pointed out as one of the challenge points despite the bandwidth advantages."* JPM: *"We continue to view the commercialization of HBF as likely to remain distant."* (JPM · Kwon, 2026-08-23)

_(Source: **Sandisk Corp, Investor Day, 2026-08-13 — Bloomberg FINAL TRANSCRIPT, company primary.** Speakers: **David Goeckeler** (Chairman/CEO), **Luis Felipe Visoso** (EVP/CFO), **Alper Ilkbahar** (EVP/CTO), **Khurram Ismali** (EVP Engineering & Product Mgmt), Ivan Donaldson (IR). Analysts on the mic: Schneider (GS), Reitzes (Melius), Newman (Bernstein), Sankar (TD Cowen), Moore (MS), Rakesh (Mizuho), Muse (Cantor), Feldman (BNP), Rakers (Wells Fargo). ⚠️ **BROKER DETECTION ERROR TO NOTE: the ingest router tagged this file "Morgan Stanley" — it latched onto Joseph Moore in the ANALYST ROSTER. This is a COMPANY primary, not a broker note.** Local copy: [HTML](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Sandisk_Corp_Investor_Day_2026813_SD000000003116400869pdf.html))_

- 🔴🔴 **ITEM 1 — MANAGEMENT'S ANSWER TO THE SUPPLY-SINK BEAR, WHICH THIS PAGE HAS CARRIED AS UNRESOLVED SINCE BERNSTEIN RAISED IT. Mizuho's Vijay Rakesh put the objection in its cleanest form:** *"one of the things we saw on DRAM side is when HBM came along, it made conventional DRAM very tight. It took out capacity. **Do you see the same thing happening with HBF where conventional NAND becomes very tight?**"* **Alper Ilkbahar's answer is a denial with a stated mechanism:** *"the beauty of HBF is that as a business, it's **entirely orthogonal to our storage business**… you have seen us talk about the **productivity of our current NAND roadmap** generating, actually giving us the **potential to easily increase our output if we choose to do so**. And this is a great outlet."* ➤ **So management's rebuttal is NOT "HBF is small" — it is "we have latent bit headroom in the existing roadmap, so HBF need not cannibalise." That is a testable claim and it is the first direct company response to the supply-sink read on this page. It cuts against Bernstein's framing and, notably, it also weakens the *bull* version of the same mechanism (HBF tightening NAND and lifting prices for everyone). Both directions of the supply-sink trade are undercut by the same sentence.** ⚠️ **No quantification of the "easily increase output" headroom was given.**
- 🔴🔴 **ITEM 2 — AND HERE IS THE ONE WITH THE WIDEST CROSS-READ, BECAUSE IT IS THE COMPANY PUTTING ITSELF ON THE SUBSTITUTION SIDE OF A DEBATE THIS PAGE SAYS IS "A LIVE SELL-SIDE DEBATE RATHER THAN A COMPANY SLIDE." Khurram Ismali, on SanDisk's own lab results:** *"I was sharing the data with David yesterday from our lab where we ran a **1.2 trillion parameter model**… we ran both the HBM sweep and DRAM sweep… And it was interesting to see to run that complex workload, **having just the HBM SSD was good enough. And you really required a MINUTE AMOUNT of DDR to run the batch services. And that was a REVELATION to us.**"* Then, unprompted, on a named competitor product: *"you heard the news that, hey, they're reducing on the **Vera Rubin**, I think **cutting the system DRAM by half, the SOCAMM DRAM**. **I would contend with the studies that we are doing, you can go even further**, right, if you have an eSSD and HBM in the system."* ➤ **Read precisely what this is and is not. It is NOT a claim that HBF displaces HBM — it is a claim that **eSSD + HBM displaces SYSTEM DRAM (DDR/SOCAMM)**, which is a different and larger target, and management is saying the already-announced Vera Rubin SOCAMM cut does not go far enough. That lands squarely on [[MU]], [[SAMSUNG]] and [[SKHYNIX]] conventional-DRAM demand, and on [[NVDA]]'s platform BOM — not on the HBM debate this page has been running. It also reframes the MS-vs-Jefferies split logged below (MS: "not zero sum… DRAM is a different use case"): management is explicitly arguing it IS partly zero-sum, just against DDR rather than HBM.** ⚠️ **Vendor lab data, self-reported, one workload, no methodology disclosed, and the speaker hedges the magnitude ("could it be reduced by half, one it depends"). Do NOT adopt as a sizing input — carry it as the supplier's directional claim, and note the obvious incentive.**
- 🔴 **ITEM 3 — THE HBF MODELLING CONSTRAINT NOBODY HAD NAMED, AND MANAGEMENT REFUSED TO ANSWER IT. Ben Reitzes (Melius) asked the exactly right question:** *"HBF. So it's **not in the model**, but you're only growing bits **mid-to high teens**. I mean, **how do we put it in? Like what if this is a hit and we got to add it to the model? What are you getting rid of that we just guided for to make room for it?** And how do we calculate upside if bits can only grow a certain amount?"* **Goeckeler's answer declines:** *"this is the conversation I think we'll have as we get the product in customers' hands… **we're not ready to go there just yet. But imagine what could be possible in the future.**"* ➤ **This SHARPENS the page's standing framing from "HBF is free optionality, not a number" to something more precise and less comfortable: HBF upside is not additive to the FY28-30 model — it competes for the SAME guided mid-to-high-teens bit CAGR unless the latent capacity in Item 1 is real. The two answers have to be held together: Reitzes asks what gets displaced, and Ilkbahar's "we can easily increase output if we choose to" is the only thing standing between HBF upside and cannibalisation of guided bits. That is now the single most important open question on this page.**
- ✅ **ITEM 4 — "100% OF EXCESS FREE CASH FLOW" IS DEFINED, ON THE RECORD, RESOLVING AN AMBIGUITY THIS PAGE HAS CARRIED IN TWO DIFFERENT WORDINGS.** Mark Newman (Bernstein) asked directly: *"on the 100% excess cash to shareholders, does that mean 100% of free cash flow? Or do you have a different definition of excess cash?"* **Visoso:** *"Excess cash is defined very simple. **The cash we're generating minus whatever we're investing in** that pocket number one, investing in the business. **There is no trick. It's 100% of the cash — excess cash — will go back to shareholders.** And if you look at what we just did in Q1, that's exactly what we did. We generated $5 billion, and we spent $4.5 billion."* ➤ **Definition settled: excess cash = cash generated less investment in the business, returned in full. The page can stop alternating between "100% of excess free cash flow" (GS) and "100% of free cash flow" — the company's own definition is the former, and the Q1 $5bn/$4.5bn print is management's own worked example.**
- **ITEM 5 — NBM COVERAGE EXTENDS BEYOND FY28, AND THE REASON IT ISN'T PUBLISHED IS DISCLOSED.** Newman asked for the FY29+ figure. Visoso: *"What is the number for '29? … **It's consistent with the numbers that you are seeing. It's consistent with the '28 number so far**, but that number will keep on evolving. **That's why we didn't put it there, because we'll keep on optimizing.**"* ➤ **Net-new: this page carries ~50% of FY27 bits and ~2/3 of FY28 bits under NBMs, ending at FY28. FY29 is guided verbally to ~the FY28 level (~two-thirds), withheld only because it is still being optimised — not because it rolls off.** On diversity, addressing the concentration risk GS raised: NBMs span *"data centers… [and] **edge customers** as well,"* and *"even when you look at the hyperscalers, their business models are dramatically different. **Diversity of business models is one of the criteria we look at.**"* Duration, in management's words: *"we have their commitment of what products they're going to deploy **quarter-by-quarter for the next 3 to 5 years**"* and *"in **2 quarters, we've gone from 3 months of visibility to 4 years of visibility**." ✅ **Corroborates SIG's "more than four years of average visibility" already on this page, from the company's own mouth.**
- **THE TWO TECHNICAL PUSHBACKS ON NAND-FOR-KV-CACHE, PUT BY TD COWEN AND ANSWERED WITH SPECS — net-new and checkable.** Krish Sankar: *"The 2 pushbacks I heard on NAND for KV cache is: one, **the tail latency is much longer than average latency**, so that impacts system throughput; and two, **it takes a long time to write KV cache onto NAND and so lowers the lifetime of the device**."* Khurram on latency: the top high-bandwidth tier *"really suffers from capacity,"* so *"you have to go to the next tiers"*; overall latency *"is actually quite good, and it's actually **workload-dependent** — if you're looking at something that is real-time, obviously you won't go to the next tier… **But you don't need to.** It's not just one or the other." On endurance: **"for the warm KV cache… that requires, like, for example, 3 drive writes per day"** and SanDisk is *"offering a **10x much higher drive write per day** as you would on our conventional SSD."* ➤ **Two hard numbers to hold: the warm-cache workload requires ~3 DWPD, and the product answer is ~10x conventional SSD endurance. That is the first quantified response on this page to the endurance objection, and it is falsifiable at product launch.**
- **MIX — MS PUT THE SUPPLY-ALLOCATION SQUEEZE DIRECTLY AND MANAGEMENT DECLINED TO SET A TARGET.** Joe Moore: *"If you're a **third data center now** and by most accounts, **data center is going to roughly double in the next 12 months**, what does that imply for your mix a year from now? Because if it's 60% data center, **you really starve the edge businesses** a lot… it seems clear to me that **there's not enough supply**."* Goeckeler: *"I think it's fair to say **our data center mix is going to go up**… we want to make sure the **whole portfolio stays robust**… **Where is the final target? It depends.**"* Plus the honest aside: *"that's a little bit of a squishy answer, but… **we know it when we see it**."* ⚠️🔴 **UNIT GUARD, because this page already carries a similar-looking number on a different base: SanDisk's OWN datacenter mix is ~1/3 (per Moore, unchallenged by management). SIG's "data center at ~50% of INDUSTRY bits in CY26 vs ~30% last year" is an INDUSTRY figure. Company mix ≠ industry bit share — do not net or compare them.**
- **HBF TECHNICAL AND CAPEX ANCHORS, from Alper — net-new:** the **HBF array technology is "mostly based on BiCS8," "so that's where the capital and installed base is based on,"** with *"the most convenient CMOS technology"* for the logic; on customisation / in-or-near-memory compute (Cantor's question): *"in or near-memory compute is going to definitely happen, and we're **entirely prepared** because we do have all the capabilities… it's **our wheelhouse**… it's entirely a **customer-based discussion** — what part of that pre-compute or the compute itself do they want to perform inside the controller that sits next to that memory."* On pricing, asked directly: *"**I feel it's priceless**"* — i.e. **declined.** ➤ **BiCS8 as the HBF array base is the useful modelling anchor: it means HBF rides installed capital rather than requiring a new node, which is consistent with Item 1's "orthogonal" claim.**
- **AND A STREET-SIDE VALUATION OBSERVATION WORTH LOGGING BECAUSE OF WHO SAID IT AND HOW MANAGEMENT REACTED.** Reitzes: *"I've been going to tech conferences and Analyst Days since about 1992, and **I've never seen a company guide for a year, 3 years out and be trading at less than 3x that number**… trading at **less than 3x your FY '30 number**."* Goeckeler's reply: *"I appreciate your observation. **I may come up with a different word than amazing for my reaction to that**, but that's a whole different discussion."* ➤ **Consistent with the ~4-5x FY30 EPS / ~$100bn cumulative FCF vs $223bn market cap arithmetic already on this page from MS. The management reaction is the tell on how they view the multiple.**
- **On the customer-driven strategy, the line that explains the whole NBM architecture:** *"we're clearly walking down a path in 2 quarters, we've gone from 3 months of visibility to 4 years of visibility"*; *"if I go back a year ago, **the incentives in this industry were just completely misaligned**… and that leads to all this volatility, and that's good for nobody"*; and on inference scaling, *"it is **the most incredible technology transition I've seen in my career by far**."* ⚠️ **Rhetoric, not a datapoint — logged for the management-commentary evolution table only.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-17 (/run-inbox) — THE *PRE*-INVESTOR-DAY READ, INGESTED LATE, AND IT SETTLES THE ONE THING THE HBF DEBATE ON THIS PAGE HAS BEEN MISSING: A CAPACITY-PER-PACKAGE NUMBER BIG ENOUGH TO HOLD **MODEL WEIGHTS**, NOT JUST KV CACHE. THAT IS THE FACT THAT DECIDES BETWEEN BERNSTEIN'S SUPPLY-SINK READ AND THE DEMAND-OPTIONALITY READ.** (**FUNDA**, "Research | Takeaways from the Future of Memory and Storage Conference 2026", **2026-08-11** — written from FMS the week BEFORE the 08-13 Investor Day and explicitly pointing readers to it: *"Investors should watch for more details at the Sandisk investor day on 13 August."* ⚠️ **DATE DISCIPLINE: this is the PRE-EVENT read. Where the Investor Day has since resolved something, the resolved version below governs and is flagged inline. Independent Substack, no rating/PT.**)
- 🔴 **THE CAPACITY CLAIM, AND IT IS THE HINGE OF THE WHOLE HBF DEBATE ON THIS PAGE:** *"HBF specs appeared to **exceed expectations**. Bandwidth and capacity both come in above HBM: **three to four HBF stacks put 1.5–2TB on one package, enough for HBF to hold model weights rather than being confined to KV cache**, which saves on networking, power, and latency."* The physical argument for why flash fits: *"Model weights are **read-only, read constantly, read in bulk, and read in a known order**. Flash suits that access pattern well."* ➜ **WHY THIS IS THE DECIDING FACT AND NOT JUST A SPEC: every HBF sizing on this page so far — SNDK's own ~1ZB persistent-KV-cache TAM (UBS, 08-10), the 4-vs-8-GPU config math, JPM's 512GB/stack — has been scoped to CACHE. Weights are a structurally different demand pool: they are resident per model instance rather than per session, so the addressable bit count scales with DEPLOYED MODELS × REPLICAS instead of with concurrent context. If the weights use case is real, Bernstein's supply-absorption mechanic (3-4x wafer capacity per exabyte, 08-14) stops being a hypothetical and becomes the base case — and, as Bernstein itself argued, that pays off for [[MU]]/[[SKHYNIX]]/[[KIOXIA]] as much as for SNDK.**
- **RECONCILIATION, NOT A CONTRADICTION — the 1.5-2TB figure is CONSISTENT with the stack-level marks already here:** Arete's **"512GB 16-die → 1.5TB gen-3"** (07-21) and JPM's **"up to 512GB on a two-stack configuration, 0.4-3.0TB/s"** (08-09). **3-4 stacks × ~500GB = 1.5-2TB.** The three sources are measuring per-die, per-stack and per-package; they agree once the basis is stated. ➜ **Use per-PACKAGE for accelerator-attach math and per-STACK for wafer math — mixing them overstates or understates by 3-4x.**
- ⚠️ **BUT ONE FUNDA-VS-FUNDA DRIFT, THREE DAYS APART, AND IT GOES THE UNFLATTERING WAY ON BANDWIDTH.** Here (08-11, pre-event): *"**Bandwidth and capacity both come in above HBM**."* In FUNDA's own post-Investor-Day note already on this page (08-14): *"Delivers **read bandwidth on par with HBM**, with 8–16x the density."* ➜ **Same house, same product, three days apart: "above HBM" became "on par with HBM" once the company put specs on a slide. The capacity claim survived the event; the bandwidth claim was walked back to parity. The 08-14 version governs — recorded because the pre-event enthusiasm is exactly the kind of mark that gets quoted later without its correction.**
- 🔴 **THE CUSTOMER SPLIT, AND IT IS THE MOST INVESTABLE LINE IN THE NOTE:** *"The Sandisk / SK Hynix announcement **confirmed Google's interest in HBF**. Investors noted **Nvidia's absence from the HBF presentations and panels**, which suggests **Nvidia prefers an architecture built on high-IOPS SSDs**."* ➜ **Both halves land on facts already on this page and sharpen them. On the positive side, [[GOOG]] confirmed is consistent with Google being a named HBF Technology Consortium member (with SK Hynix, Tenstorrent and, per BofA 08-13, now Meta). On the negative side, NVIDIA is conspicuously NOT a consortium member, and its own named storage paths are SCADA (GPU-direct-to-eSSD) and CMX (GPU→DPU→eSSD) — high-IOPS SSD architectures, not accelerator-attached flash. So the HBF bull case currently runs through the ASIC/TPU camp and NOT through the largest accelerator buyer on the tape. That is a materially narrower anchor-demand story than "the consortium is expanding" implies — and it is the demand-side version of @bubbleboi's "who is actually the customer" objection already logged on [SKHYNIX](SKHYNIX.md).**
- **The supply mechanic, dated EARLIER than the quantified version this page carries:** *"**If HBF ramps, it may consume NAND wafers at premium pricing the same way HBM did inside DRAM. Every wafer diverted to HBF then tightens commodity flash supply.**"* ➜ **Chronology worth recording: FUNDA states the mechanism on 08-11; Bernstein QUANTIFIES it on 08-14 (3-4x wafer capacity per exabyte, ~4x die count/wafer area vs a ~128GB die). The page's Changelog frames the Bernstein version as "NET-NEW, HBF RE-FRAMED FROM DEMAND OPTION TO SUPPLY ABSORPTION" — accurate as to the page, but the re-framing was in the market three days earlier and unquantified. Bernstein gets the number; FUNDA gets the priority.**
- **On timing FUNDA is now SUPERSEDED by the event, and the direction is neutral-to-worse:** *"We heard that **2027 is the target for HBF samples**, though there were **few details on the technical obstacles** that need to be cleared before commercialization."* ➜ **The Investor Day confirmed 2027 samples and added the gap that matters: NO commercial-shipment timeline and HBF explicitly OUTSIDE the FY28-30 model. So the pre-event expectation was met on samples and disappointed on commercialisation — consistent with the Jefferies desk's *"relatively lackluster/disappointing"* read and against Jefferies research's *"even more compelling."* The page's standing framing survives unchanged: HBF is free optionality, not a number.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/fundamemory.html)

**🆕⚠️ 2026-08-17 (/run-inbox) — THESIS DRIFT: MIZUHO CUTS SNDK TO $1,900 FROM $2,200 (−13.6%) WHILE STAYING OUTPERFORM — AND IT IS THE FIRST HOUSE TO RE-MARK THE NAME SINCE THE INVESTOR DAY, WHICH RETIRES ONE OF THE THREE "STALE" ENTRIES THIS PAGE HAS BEEN FLAGGING.** (**Mizuho · Vijay Rakesh** (w/ Dale Gai, Kevin Wang), "AI Server Call: 2027 VR with HBM and NPO/CPO, Adv. Packaging EMIB-T/CoPoS Ramps", **2026-08-16**. **Outperform, PT $1,900**, px **$1,641.11**. Prior: **$2,200, 2026-07-11** — see [## Changelog](#changelog).)
- **Where $1,900 sits in the post-Investor-Day ladder:** **SIG $2,745 → BofA $2,500 → JPM $2,250 → GS $2,200 → Citi $2,100 → 🆕 Mizuho $1,900 → Jefferies = MS $1,750.** ➜ **It lands inside the $1,750-$2,100 gap the page has repeatedly flagged as EMPTY — so the "nobody in between" observation is now, narrowly, false: Mizuho is the first mark between the acyclicality camp and the long-shortage camp. ⚠️ Read that carefully: it is a PT cut into a ~14% lower share price, so on the multiple it is not necessarily a downgrade of the thesis. The note is an AI-server industry piece, not an SNDK re-underwrite — SNDK appears in the rating/PT table and in the "positive for LITE/MU/SNDK" HBM/NPO-CPO read-through, with no SNDK-specific model detail disclosed. Logged as a coverage mark, NOT as new fundamental work.**
- **The transmission channel Mizuho names for SNDK is the memory/HBM leg, not NAND pricing:** VR-Ultra driving *"strong NPO/CPO adoption and upside to HBM (positive for LITE/MU/SNDK)"*, with **HBM4e pricing up 70-100%** and Feynman HBM *"potentially constrained as **LTAs/SCAs/NBMs determine pricing**."* ➜ **That last clause is the one that matters here, because it is a sell-side house putting SanDisk's NBMs in the same sentence as Micron's SCAs as PRICE-SETTING instruments for 2028+ accelerator memory. This page's core structural claim is that the two are NOT the same instrument — SNDK's NBMs are a FLOOR with the uncontracted third floating, Micron's first 16 SCAs carry a CQ2 CEILING. Mizuho does not distinguish them. ⚠️ Flagged as a conflation in the source, not adopted; the distinction is the cleanest structural differentiator on this page and it survives.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/AI_Server_Call__2027_VR_with_HBM_and_NPO_CPO_Adv_Packaging.html)

**🆕🔴 2026-08-15 — THE PRIMARY LANDS: the 98-page Investor Day deck itself is now on disk, and it SETTLES three disputes this page has been carrying on second-hand broker readings. Two of the page's own "flagged, not adopted" warnings turn out to have been wrong about the source, and one broker (Citi) is off by a full fiscal year.** (SanDisk Corporation, "Investor Day 2026" company presentation, event 2026-08-13, filed 2026-08-14 — **company primary**.)
- ✅ **RESOLVED #1 — the SIG-vs-BofA "arithmetic tension" on bit-per-wafer productivity was never a tension. Both numbers are on the SAME SLIDE, and both are the company's.** The deck reads: *"**27% CAGR Yearly Average Productivity**"* alongside *"**BiCS Gen-to-Gen Average Bit Growth Per Wafer — 54%**"*, under the header *"54% AVERAGE GEN-TO-GEN, BIT PER WAFER GROWTH OVER 5 GENERATIONS"* across BiCS5→BiCS6→BiCS8→BiCS10→BiCS11 (Source: Sandisk internal data). ➜ **SIG's ~27%/year and BofA's ~54%/generation are the same roadmap on two bases, printed side by side. Neither house erred. The page's standing instruction not to NET them stands; the accusation that one of them mis-stated the deck is withdrawn.**
- ✅ **RESOLVED #2 — and this one is a correction AGAINST this page.** The page flagged that *"BofA's two growth rates look transposed relative to the 'data center overtakes consumer' claim they accompany."* The deck shows they were not transposed: the slide *"FLASH MARKET TO REACH 1.2ZB IN 2026 — IN 2026 DATA CENTER OVERTAKES EDGE AS THE LARGEST FLASH SWIMLANE"* splits the chart into a **historical** era, *"Growth in Consumer & Edge (**30%+ CAGR**)"* (drivers: phone volumes to 1.5Bu by the mid-2010s, PCs shifting HDD→flash, enterprise SSD emergence), and a **forward** era, *"Growth Driven by AI/DC Buildout (**High-Teens CAGR**)"* (drivers: AI buildout takes DC to the majority of the market; premium mix, not unit volume, carries growth). ⚠️ **The real caveat is different from the one the page raised: the two rates are different ERAS, not different segments in one period. Quoting "Consumer/Edge 30%+ vs DC high-teens" as a contemporaneous comparison — as BofA's phrasing invites — is the actual error. Use SIG's bit-share figures for a same-period statement, as the page already advises.**
- 🔴 **DISPUTE #3 — CITI IS A FULL FISCAL YEAR EARLY ON NBM COVERAGE, AND THE PRIMARY SETTLES IT AGAINST CITI.** The deck states plainly: *"**50% FY27 Supply under NBMs** with Committed Economics… **Growing to approximately 67% in FY28**"*, with contract length *"**1 to 5 years, with average weighted length of 4+ years**"* and *"bigger customers have longer commitments."* Citi's note instead says coverage is *"50% of bits covered in FY26, ~66% in FY27."* ➜ **BofA, SIG, JPM, Bernstein and the deck all say FY27/FY28. Citi's FY26/FY27 is a one-year shift and is NOT adopted; the page keeps 50% FY27 → ~67% FY28.** (Citi's forward claim that NBMs cover *"~66-70% of bit shipments through FY29-FY30"* is net-new and has no primary contradiction — logged as Citi's, unconfirmed.)
  - ✅🔴 **RESOLVED 2026-08-18 IN THIS PAGE'S FAVOUR — AND CITI FIXED IT SILENTLY. Citi has REISSUED the identical note under an 08-18 date, and the ONLY substantive change in the entire document is this sentence: it now reads "50% of bits covered in FY27, ~66% in FY28."** (Verified by full-text diff of the two PDFs: the reissue differs from the 08-14 original in exactly two places — this fiscal-year correction and a capitalisation change in "Target Price". **Buy and the $2,100 PT are unchanged; no estimate, no thesis, and no other number moved.**) ➤ **Worth stating plainly because it is the cleanest validation of the ingest discipline on this wiki: on 08-14 this page refused to adopt a sell-side number because it contradicted the company's own deck, logged the refusal as a named dispute, and four days later the broker corrected to the page's figure without announcing it. The rule that produced the win — when a broker note and a company primary disagree on a disclosed fact, the primary wins and the disagreement gets logged rather than averaged — is the one to keep applying.** ⚠️ **Operational note: a corrected reissue carries the SAME title and the SAME filename as the original, so it looks like a duplicate to any dedupe-by-name step. It was caught here only by diffing against the copy in `_done\`. Never drop a same-title re-arrival without a content diff first.** (Citi · "Sandisk Corp (SNDK.O): Bullish Outlook; Focus on Driving…", Buy, PT $2,100, reissued 2026-08-18.) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260818_Citi_SNDK_Sandisk_Corp_-SNDKO--_Bullish_Outlook-_Focus_on_Driving.html)
- **NET-NEW FROM THE PRIMARY — the supply slide, with the actual wafer numbers behind JPM's "~30% below prior peak at ~100% utilization" claim:** *"CAPACITY PEAKED IN CY22, TROUGHED TO ~70% UTILIZATION IN THE CORRECTION, RE-ESTABLISHING CAPACITY ~30% BELOW PEAK"* — **wafer capacity peaked ~1,830 kwpm; ~500 kwpm went idle at the ~70%-utilisation trough; ~560 kwpm was RETIRED; the remainder now runs at ~100% utilisation.** ➜ **The "~30% below peak" figure is a RETIREMENT, not idling — ~560 kwpm of capacity is gone, not mothballed. That distinction is the whole argument for why this cycle's supply response is slower than prior cycles, and it was not visible in any of the six broker recaps.**
- **NET-NEW — the 2030 AI-DC TAM split, from the primary, WITH ITS PROVENANCE (which matters):** **1.2ZB 2030 AI DC TAM — 40% Staging / 35% KV Cache / 25% Fast Data Lakes; NAND technology split TLC 66% / QLC 34%.** ⚠️ **Read the footnotes: the 1.2ZB TAM is sourced to "TechInsights NAND Market Report Q2 2026" — a THIRD PARTY — while the workload splits are "Sandisk internal splits."** ➜ **This exactly reproduces the Citrini thread already on this page (480EB / 420EB / 300EB), which is simply those percentages × 1.2ZB. Citrini is CONFIRMED as a faithful reading of the deck and is not an independent estimate — do not treat the two as corroborating sources. The unit guard already on the page (annual demand ≠ the ~1ZB installed base) is unaffected.**
- **NET-NEW — the JV's scale, third-party sourced:** *"**JV is the Largest Producer of NAND Wafers in the World (33%)**"* (Source: TechInsights NAND Market Report Q2 2026), *"JV Extended through 2034."* And the Nanya DRAM investment is quantified as an equity position for the first time on this page: **~4% equity stake**, to *"Secure DRAM for eSSD Portfolio."* Reads onto [[KIOXIA]].

**🆕🔴 2026-08-15 — TWO HOUSES THIS PAGE WAS MISSING, AND ONE OF THEM BREAKS THE PAGE'S OWN "9-10x CY27" FRAMING OF THE PT LADDER. The Street-high $2,745 is NOT struck off CY27 at all.**
- 🔴 **CORRECTION TO THIS PAGE — SIG's PT basis.** The page asserts that *"the $2,200-$2,745 PT cluster resolves to 9-10x CY27 EPS of ~$250-255."* The SIG note says otherwise, verbatim: *"**Our price target of $2,745 is based on 11x FY28 EPS**, 7x EV/sales, 8x EV/EBITDA, 5x PB, and 6x TBV."* SIG's tearsheet carries **FY27e EPS $222.38 and FY28e EPS $249.43** (revenue $51.4bn / $56.6bn; P/E 6.0x / 5.4x) — and **11 × $249.43 = $2,744**, which reproduces the target exactly. ➜ **So the top of the ladder is 11x an FY28 number, not 9-10x a CY27 number. The correct statement is that JPM ($2,250 = 9x CY27 $250), BofA ($2,500 = 10x C27 $255) and Citi ($2,100 = 9x CY27) form a genuine 9-10x CY27 cluster, while SIG sits ABOVE it on BOTH axes — a higher multiple AND a further-out year. That materially weakens the page's "FUNDA's $292 at the sell-side's multiple ≈ the top of the ladder" bridge, because the top of the ladder is not built the way the page said it was.** See [## Changelog](#changelog). (Susquehanna · Mehdi Hosseini / Bastien Faucon-Morin, "SanDisk: Not All NANDs Are Created Equal, and Why Deployment Matters for KC Caching," 2026-08-13.)
- **🆕 CITI IS NEW TO THIS PAGE AND IT IS THE LOWEST-EPS HOUSE IN THE POST-DAY SET — while simultaneously being the most bullish on the out-year versus consensus.** **Buy reiterated, TP $2,100 (9x CY27E EPS), px $1,528.11 (13 Aug), market cap $223.1bn.** The fiscal ladder (FY June): **FY26A $70.99 (cons $70.88) · FY27E $205.36 vs First Call consensus $218.73 · FY28E $268.28 vs cons $254.15 · FY29E $265.18 vs cons $199.12.** ⚠️ **UNIT/BASIS GUARD — the same FY-vs-CY trap this wiki has hit before: Citi's TABLE is FISCAL (June year-end) but the TARGET is struck off CALENDAR CY27. Do not read Citi's CY27 EPS as $205.36. Rolling Citi's own quarters into calendar 2027 (Mar-27 $50.61 + Jun-27 $57.56 + Sep-27 $64.52 + Dec-27 $71.31) gives ~$244, which at the stated 9x implies ~$2,196 — modestly above the printed $2,100, so even Citi's own arithmetic does not close cleanly. Logged as published; the stated basis is "9x CY27E."** ➜ **The genuinely useful line is the consensus comparison: Citi is 6% BELOW the Street on FY27 and 33% ABOVE it on FY29 ($265.18 vs $199.12). That is the same shape as Jefferies' "consensus has FY30 revenue -30%" observation already on this page — the Street's disagreement with this story is concentrated entirely in the out-years, not in the next twelve months.** (Citi · Asiya Merchant / Michael Cadiz, "Sandisk Corp — Bullish Outlook; Focus on Driving Sustainable Margins, Reducing Cyclicality & Shareholder Returns," 2026-08-14.)
- **🆕 BERNSTEIN REITERATES THE STREET-HIGH $3,000, AND ITS CASE IS NOW ALMOST ENTIRELY A SHARE-COUNT CASE.** *"Using even the most conservative of estimates for Free cash flow, Sandisk can **buyback 47% of shares outstanding in the next 4 years**."* The mechanics, spelled out: applying the company's 50% FCF margin to Bernstein's revenue gives **FCF of $25.0bn FY27 / $28.4bn FY28 / $25.0bn FY29 / $26.5bn FY30 = $104.9bn cumulative**; at a constant **$1,500** repurchase price that retires **~69.9m shares, taking the count from 148.09m to ~78.1m by FY30 (−47%)**, which *"holding net income constant… would increase EPS by approximately 90%."* At a constant **$3,000** (its own target) the buyback still retires ~35.0m shares to ~113.1m (**−24%**). (Bernstein · Mark C. Newman / April Li / Phoebe Sun, "Memory & Storage: Why Sandisk investor day makes us more bullish on SNDK; Reiterate Outperform TP $3000," 2026-08-14.)
- 🔴 **⚠️ BUT BERNSTEIN'S OWN REVENUE LINE CONTRADICTS THE COMPANY FRAMEWORK IT IS ENDORSING, AND NOBODY ELSE ON THIS PAGE HAS THIS. Bernstein models FY27 $50.0bn → FY28 $56.8bn → FY29 $50.1bn → FY30 $53.0bn — i.e. a −11.9% REVENUE DECLINE IN FY29 and FY30 still below FY28.** Management guided **mid-to-high-teens revenue growth every year FY28-30**. ➜ **So the Street-high target is underwritten by a model that quietly rejects the growth half of the long-term framework and replaces it with buyback accretion off a cyclical down-year. That is a far more defensible bull case than it first appears — it does not need acyclicality — but it is NOT the case Bernstein's own headline ("makes us more bullish") advertises, and it means the $3,000 and the company's FY28-30 model are not the same bet. Flagged prominently: anyone citing "Bernstein $3,000" as endorsement of management's growth targets is misreading the note.**
- **NET-NEW ON HBF, AND IT IS THE MOST CONSEQUENTIAL SUPPLY CLAIM IN THE SET — HBF CONSUMES WAFERS RATHER THAN SAVING THEM.** Bernstein: HBF *"would also require **3x to 4x the wafer capacity (in our estimates) per Exabyte** due to the additional space required on-chip. This could **significantly eat up wafer supply and lead to shortages continuing far longer than even the bulls expect**,"* elaborating that versus a conventional ~128GB die, *"producing HBF may require roughly **4x the die count and wafer area**"*, which raises HBF's *"share of effective industry wafer capacity relative to the bits delivered, perpetuating supply tightness and supporting pricing."* ➜ **This inverts how HBF has been framed on this page. Every prior mention treated HBF as a DEMAND call option ("free optionality, not a number"). Bernstein makes it a SUPPLY-ABSORPTION mechanism: HBF succeeding would tighten the whole NAND market, not just add a product line. That is the first HBF argument on this page that pays off even for holders of MU/SKHYNIX rather than only for SNDK.** Cross-ref [MU.md](MU.md), [SKHYNIX.md](SKHYNIX.md), [themes/hbm-memory.md](themes/hbm-memory.md).
- **Two smaller net-new NBM details from Bernstein, both incrementally supportive of durability:** *"The **coverage ratio of financial guarantees as a portion of remaining contract value increases over the contract life (2x in 2 years)** to give much more protection in the outer years when it is most needed"*, and — the one that is genuinely new evidence rather than structure — *"**2 of the early customers came back to renew and increase the NBMs**."* ➜ **A renewal-and-increase is the first observable test of the NBM model that has actually occurred, as opposed to being contractually promised. Small, but it is evidence rather than terms.**
- **⚠️ A DISSENTING VOICE ON HBF FROM OUTSIDE THE MEMORY DESKS, worth carrying because the HBF debate on this page has become a bull-vs-more-bull argument.** UBS's semicap analyst, asked directly whether NAND takes off on HBF: *"I skew a little bit **skeptical on HBF**. I mean, SanDisk has been talking about this for, I don't know, **two years** now… **I don't see a lot of evidence that it's really taking off**"* (UBS · Timothy Arcuri, sector-sales callback hosted by Robert Ruple, 2026-08-14). ➜ **Not a memory analyst and not a rating — but it is the only flatly skeptical HBF read in the post-Investor-Day set, and it comes from someone who tracks the equipment orders that would have to precede an HBF ramp.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-14/15 — THE INDEPENDENT (NON-BROKER) RESEARCH LANDS TWO DAYS AFTER THE ANALYST DAY, AND IT SITS FAR ABOVE THE ENTIRE SELL-SIDE ON EARNINGS. FUNDA MODELS CY2027/28/29 EPS OF $292 / $334 / $388 — versus JPM's CY27 $250 and BofA's C27 $255, the two numbers the top of the PT ladder is built on.**
- **The marks (FUNDA, "Research | SNDK Investor Day 2026: Focus Shifts to Earnings Sustainability," 2026-08-14):** *"Under this base case, we project **CY2027/28/29 EPS of $292/$334/$388**, implying forward **P/E multiples of 5.2x/4.6x/3.9x** at the current share price."* Base-case assumptions stated: *"we currently model a **mild pricing pullback in CY2028** and a **mid-to-high-teens bit shipment CAGR for FY2028–FY2030**, in line with the company's long-term bit supply growth target."*
- 🔴 **WHY THE GAP MATTERS MORE THAN THE NUMBER: FUNDA is ~17% above JPM on CY27 EPS while assuming the SAME bit-growth target and a CY2028 price PULLBACK — i.e. it is not more bullish on the cycle, it is more bullish on the earnings conversion. That makes it a clean test of the sell-side's CY27 EPS base rather than a louder version of the same call.** ⚠️ **FUNDA is an independent Substack research shop, not a broker; no rating, no PT, and the estimates are unaudited. Logged as a third mark on the CY27 EPS question, NOT adopted, and NOT to be averaged with the sell-side.** ➜ **Practical use: the $2,200-$2,745 PT cluster resolves to 9-10x CY27 EPS of ~$250-255. On FUNDA's $292 the same multiples give $2,630-$2,920 — so the top of the existing ladder is roughly FUNDA's number at the sell-side's multiple. The dispersion on this page is an EPS disagreement wearing a multiple disagreement's clothes.**
- **The mechanism FUNDA underwrites is the one this page already tracks, restated with a number:** *"Roughly **50% to two-thirds of total supply is protected under NBMs by FY2028**; the **remaining uncommitted capacity floats with prevailing market prices, which preserves earnings leverage in an upcycle**."* And on the floor: *"**Even at the minimum floor price, non-GAAP gross margin is expected to hold at ~80%**."* ➜ **Consistent with BofA/SIG/JPM. The incremental framing is the second half — the NBM book is a floor, and the un-contracted third is the call option.**
- **On HBF, FUNDA's version is the most specific technical claim on the page:** *"Delivers **read bandwidth on par with HBM, with 8–16x the density**. The **first silicon has completed tape-out**, with **customer sampling targeted for 2027**. **HBF sits OUTSIDE the FY2028–FY2030 long-term financial model targets so that commercialization would be pure incremental upside.**"*
- ⚠️🔴 **AND HERE IS A SAME-FIRM CONTRADICTION ON HBF THAT SHOULD NOT BE SMOOTHED: this page carries the Jefferies DESK read (Rankie Wong, 08-13) that HBF updates were *"relatively lackluster/disappointing… clearly seems like it's still relatively far away."* Jefferies RESEARCH (Blayne Curtis, 08-14) says the opposite: *"**The HBF presentation was EVEN MORE COMPELLING, highlighting emerging inference opportunities.**"* Same firm, same event, opposite verdicts, one day apart. Both retained as published — the desk and the analyst are not the same source, and this page's standing framing ("HBF is free optionality, not a number") survives either way.**
- ⚠️ **A SECOND FLAG IN THE SAME SENTENCE OF THE SAME NOTE, AND IT IS A MISCHARACTERISATION WORTH CATCHING: Curtis writes that *"SanDisk introduced an **80% THROUGH-CYCLE GM target** at Analyst Day."* It did not. The ~80% GM is a **three-year FY28–30 model**, and the page already logs that "no through-cycle model — only three years" was the LOUDEST PUSHBACK on the day. Calling it "through-cycle" concedes to management exactly the acyclicality claim MS explicitly refuses. Flagged, not adopted.** (Jefferies · Blayne Curtis, "Blayne's Bytes," 2026-08-14.)

**🆕🔴 2026-08-14 — THE DEMAND SIDE OF THE INVESTOR DAY, DECOMPOSED — and the 2030 data-centre split is now on the page in exabytes.** (Citrini Research · "Citrini Semis" subscriber thread, 2026-08-14 — buy-side/independent, no rating or PT.)
- **The bit-demand path:** *"SanDisk estimates bit shipments to grow to **1.2ZB (1200EB) in 2026, up from 960EB in 2025**. **Data Centers will roughly contribute 50% of the bit demand.**"* ➜ **+25% industry bit growth 2025→2026, and the ~50% DC share corroborates SIG's "data center at ~50% of industry bits in CY26 vs ~30% last year" already on this page. Two independent readers of the same deck agree on the share.**
- 🔴 **THE 2030 DECOMPOSITION, WHICH IS NET-NEW AND IS THE PART WORTH KEEPING:** *"They also expect **data center-related demand to reach 1.2ZB (1200EB) by 2030**, with **480EB for staging, 420EB for KV cache, and 300EB for fast data lakes**. **Staging and hotter KV cache will be handled by higher-performance but lower-capacity TLC NAND, while fast data lakes and colder KV cache will be stored in QLC NAND.** They expect **TLC NAND to be dominant and contribute around 66% of the total bits, while 34% of the total bits are QLC NAND.**"* ⚠️ **UNIT GUARD: this page already carries "KV cache: ~1ZB persistent INSTALLED BASE by 2030, ~35% of the NAND market" from the Investor Day. Citrini's 420EB is KV cache inside 1.2ZB of ANNUAL DC DEMAND. An installed base and an annual demand figure are different quantities — do NOT reconcile 1ZB against 420EB, and do not net them.**
- **The spot check, which is the only real-time datapoint in the thread:** *"**TLC NAND spot prices have finally started RISING from mid-July after falling continuously since the March peak** as we come closer to **Rubin mass production**. Meanwhile, **QLC NAND spot prices have been FLAT** for a while."* ➜ **A TLC/QLC divergence is the observable that would distinguish "staging + hot KV cache is ramping" from "the whole NAND tape is inflating." It leans the first way.**
- **Citrini's own overlay, clearly an opinion:** *"I personally think that their **data center NAND demand forecast for 2030 is quite CONSERVATIVE**, as they **haven't modeled persistent agent memory**, which is going to be the biggest driver of NAND demand as long-horizon agents are released to the general public next year."* ⚠️ **Unquantified, unsourced, and from an anonymous buy-side handle — logged as the bull-side extension of the KV-cache thesis, not as a number.**

**🆕 2026-08-14 — POSITIONING IS THE BULL CASE THIS WEEK, AND TWO DESKS INDEPENDENTLY SAY THE SAME THING: MEMORY DID NOT PARTICIPATE IN THE AI RE-GROSS.**
- **MS (Sales · Skyler Risom, "Friday Five," 2026-08-14, relaying MS Prime Brokerage + Shawn Kim):** *"**Positioning looks CLEANER in Memory** for the next leg… investors **de-grossed in Memory the most in the unwind (late June–late July)… and have CONTINUED TO REDUCE EXPOSURE FURTHER even as other AI sectors have come back on**."* On the fundamental side: *"While **Shawn Kim still sees classic 'LATE-CYCLE' characteristics** in the Memory trade (i.e. **price increases can't hold 7x historical peak forever**), he thinks that **3x NTM P/E suggests that markets UNDERESTIMATE THE DURATION of Memory tightness**."* ➜ **Note the shape of that argument: it concedes the bear's mechanism (late cycle, unsustainable price level) and disputes only the TIMING — which is the same "long shortage, not acyclicality" position MS takes on SNDK specifically. Internally consistent.** MS also credits the day: SNDK *"delivered on one of the core tenets of Shawn Kim's call for re-rating in Memory: **a buyback (100% FCF to shareholders)**,"* and flags **generalist L/S funds bought Memory at 2.3x the level TMT funds did** (off a lower base). Price context: **SNDK +35% on the week.**
- **UBS (Sector Sales · Robert Ruple, 2026-08-15) says the same from the other side of the Street:** *"**Memory remains the most controversial.** I sense that **hedge funds continue to lean tactically SHORT**, with debates centered around whether **LTAs have capped upside, NAND fundamentals are weakening, and capital returns are appropriate despite the industry's long-term cyclicality**. **Yet market action tells a different story.** Samsung (+16.5% 5D), SK Hynix (+13.7%), Micron (+10.5%), and **SanDisk (+35%)** all rallied sharply, with **SNDK's Investor Day helping reinforce confidence in a more stable earnings framework.**"* ➜ **The three named bear debates map exactly onto this page's open questions. Nothing new in substance — the value is that the buy-side's objections are now enumerated by a desk that talks to them.**
- **Price context from a third desk (BofA Sales · Nicholas Villani, 2026-08-14), relaying Wamsi Mohan (Buy, PT $2,500):** analyst *"exited the Analyst Day very upbeat"* and sees *"**another >60% upside from here**."* The tape: **+51% off the lows in the past 2–3 weeks, still −34% off the highs, +544% on the year.** ⚠️ **No PT change — this restates the existing $2,500. Logged for the drift record and the price context, not as a new mark.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-14 — THE FULL ANALYST-DAY NOTE SET, AND THE HEADLINE IS A NEW RATING: JPM COMES OFF RESTRICTION STRAIGHT TO OVERWEIGHT WITH A $2,250 DEC-27 TARGET. But the more useful note is the one that disagrees — MORGAN STANLEY, WHICH IS OW, SAYS PLAINLY THAT IT DOES *NOT* BUY MANAGEMENT'S "STRUCTURAL NOT CYCLICAL" FRAMING AND OWNS THE STOCK ANYWAY. That is the cleanest articulation of the bull case that does not require the acyclicality thesis to be true.**

| House / analyst | Rating | PT | Basis |
|---|---|---:|---|
| **JPM · Harlan Sur** | **🆕 OVERWEIGHT (from Not Rated)** | **$2,250 (Dec-27)** | **9x CY27E EPS $250** — midpoint of the historical **7–10x forward memory P/E** range; ~47% upside from $1,528.11 |
| **Barclays · Tom O'Malley** | OW (reit) | **$2,300 (unch)** | +50.5% from $1,528.11 |
| **Morgan Stanley · Joe Moore** | OW (reit) | **$1,750 (unch)** | — |
| **Jefferies · Blayne Curtis** | BUY (reit) | **$1,750 (unch)** | +15%; Cal-26 EPS **$160.30** |
| **GS · James Schneider** | Buy (reit) | — | — |
| **🆕 Citi · Asiya Merchant** | Buy (reit) | **$2,100 (unch)** | **9x CY27E EPS**; peer set 3-4x EV/sales & 9-10x P/E, Kioxia median 8x, larger peer group 6-8x — "SanDisk's LTAs merit a higher multiple vs peers"; +37.4% from $1,528.11 |
| **🆕 Bernstein · Mark C. Newman** | Outperform (reit) | **$3,000 (unch)** | Street high; buyback-accretion case (see below) |

- **🔴 JPM's INITIATION IS THE FIRST FULL THESIS BUILT ON THE NBM FRAMEWORK RATHER THAN ON THE CYCLE, and it restates the contract numbers with one that is new to this page: *"8 signed NBMs representing **~$94B in total contract value at floor pricing**, weighted-average contract duration of **4+ years**, and **~80% GM even at the floor pricing tier**"*, with *"contracts detailing **month-by-month volume, mix, and price commitments** backed by **$16.5B in financial guarantees**, significantly improving forward visibility from the '**three months of pricing visibility**' that has been 'standard operating procedure' for the industry, to **4+ years of committed economics**."* Sur's judgement on the durability question this page has been litigating since June: *"**To us this screens as a substantive reset rather than peak-cycle pricing capture**"*, and *"we expect **variable pricing to trend above the floor across most of the contract life** given current supply/demand dynamics."* On the earnings math: *"mid-/high-teens% revenue growth ultimately translates into an **EPS growth CAGR north of 25%**"*, amplified by *"an **aggressive share count reduction trajectory**."* CY27 EPS **$250**. **HBF and 3D Matrix Memory are framed as *"meaningful virtual call options on inference-era memory architecture"* — explicitly NOT in the numbers**, consistent with every other house. (JPM · Harlan Sur / Mayur Ramdhani / Apoorva Kumar, 2026-08-14)
- **🔴 THE SUPPLY-SIDE CLAIM THAT DOES THE MOST WORK IN JPM'S THESIS, AND IT IS NEW TO THIS PAGE:** *"**industry wafer capacity now ~30% below prior peak and running at ~100% utilization**. This means **near-term bit growth comes primarily from nodal migrations rather than wafer additions**, which structurally supports pricing durability and, we think, **extends the current upcycle materially beyond what historical patterns would suggest**."* And the management datapoint that carries it: *"mgmt indicated **bits are expected to remain on allocation BEYOND CY27** — a data point we think is materially bullish for sustained NAND pricing durability through the remainder of the decade."* ⚠️ **Note the exact tension with the page's standing Risks section: MS's Asia desk (07-28) has industry NAND bit growth moving from high-teens to 20-25% next year with YMTC adding a third 50k-wafer line. "~30% below prior peak at ~100% utilization" and "20-25% industry bit growth" are not contradictory — one is wafers, one is bits off node migration — but they underwrite opposite conclusions about how tight 2027 actually is. Both logged; neither adopted as the page's supply base case.**
- **🔴 MS IS THE ONE THAT SHOULD BE READ MOST CAREFULLY, BECAUSE IT IS AN OW THAT REJECTS THE COMPANY'S CENTRAL CLAIM.** *"We aren't completely in agreement with management's view that **improvements are more structural than cyclical**… Our view is a little bit more focused on cyclicality — **we don't see 75%+ operating margins being sustainable; that's happening because there is a shortage**. But with **20% supply growth amid much stronger data center growth**, and shortages through other parts of the memory/storage hierarchy (both hard disk drives and DRAM), **we can stay at or above these margin levels for multiple years**."* And on the contracts: *"it's **hard to tell how these new contracts will perform over a cycle** — but **it doesn't matter to the shorter-term investment case as long as NAND is in shortage**, where we have very high conviction that it will continue to be as long as AI investment continues at this pace."* ➜ **This is the honest bull case and it is materially different from JPM's: MS is underwriting a LONG SHORTAGE, not an ACYCLICAL BUSINESS. On MS's construction the NBMs are a nice-to-have; on JPM's they are the thesis. That distinction is now the sharpest fault line on this page and it should be watched at the first sign of NAND price rollover — the two theses fail at different times.**
- **⚠️ MS ALSO EXPLAINS WHY THE ~80% GM NUMBER WAS THE *RIGHT* ANSWER, AND IT IS A POSITIONING POINT, NOT A FUNDAMENTAL ONE:** *"We heard a consistent investor concern coming into the investor day that Sandisk stock could react negatively to a long-term model, given a **higher gross margin forecast than the current 84% would be seen as unrealistic** and a **lower one evidence there's limited upside to ASP/margins going forward**. We thought **management threaded the needle**, with the target model referring only to FY28-30, and a margin level corresponding to **what we already knew was the NBM margin floor**."* ➜ **The ~80% guide was never a forecast — it was the floor, restated as a target. That is the most important structural read of the day and it reframes the "peak margin" debate: the company guided to its DOWNSIDE, not its base case.**
- **💰 THE CASH MATH, FROM TWO INDEPENDENT HOUSES, AND IT IS THE PART THAT MOVED THE STOCK.** MS: FY28–30 model *"putting the stock at about **~4-5x FY30 EPS**, with cumulative FCF between now and then of **~$100bn vs the current market cap of $223bn**."* Mechanics, disclosed: *"In **Q4 2026, Sandisk generated $5 billion of free cash flow and bought back $4.5 billion of stock — and investors should expect that going forward**. The current authorization has **$15.5 billion remaining**"* (MS, 2026-08-14). Jefferies reconciles the authorisation history exactly: *"generated **$5B of adjusted free cash flow in the June quarter and returned $4.5 billion**, leaving **$1.5 billion on the original $6 billion** authorization, and the board authorized an **incremental $14 billion** for **$15.5 billion of total unspent capacity**"* (Jefferies · Curtis, 2026-08-13). ✅ **This RESOLVES the 08-05 flag on this page where Jefferies said $14bn incremental and TMTB said $15bn: $14bn incremental was correct, and $15.5bn is the total remaining. See [## Changelog](#changelog).**
- **🔴 CAPITAL EFFICIENCY IS THE MOST UNDER-QUOTED DISCLOSURE OF THE DAY, AND IT IS A COMPETITIVE-MOAT NUMBER, NOT A FINANCIAL ONE.** MS: *"Across **BiCS5 through BiCS10 (5 nodes over ~9 years)**, Sandisk has delivered a **27% annual CAGR in bit output per wafer**… The **Sandisk/Kioxia JV has spent 2.66x LESS capital than the industry average per incremental petabyte of output in 2025**, and **over 2021–2025 produced 29% of industry bits while spending only 13% of industry capex (~2x the capital efficiency of the nearest competitor)**"* (MS, 2026-08-14). ➜ **Reads directly onto [[KIOXIA]] (same JV, same fabs) and is the strongest available rebuttal to the "NAND always over-builds" bear leg — this pair has structurally under-spent the industry for five years.**
- **BiCS9 IS THE PART THAT IS ALREADY SHIPPING, AND IT IS CUSTOM.** MS: *"**BiCS9, announced just last week, is the first product to apply the CBA hybrid-bonding 'derivative roadmap'**, which combines the BiCS8 cell array with next-gen CMOS to deliver a big performance step-up with **minimal incremental capital**. The new node is **designed specifically to hyperscaler specs and already shipping as part of Sandisk's NBM agreements**"* (2026-08-14). **BiCS10 (332-layer 2Tb QLC) is sampling as of Aug 2026** — JPM notes it is *"sampling **ahead of schedule**"* with *"**65% more bits per wafer than BiCS8**"*, and adds the spec deltas vs BiCS8: **~60% memory density, ~100% write/read bandwidth, ~33% interface speed, ~75% power efficiency**. ➜ **A hyperscaler-custom node shipping inside the NBM book is a materially stronger disclosure than a roadmap slide: it means the contracts are attached to a specific, differentiated part, not to commodity bits.**
- **⚠️ HBF — THE ONE PLACE THE HOUSES SPLIT, AND JEFFERIES IS THE OUTLIER IN THE BULLISH DIRECTION.** Barclays: *"**HBF memory die has been taped out with product samples expected in CY27, but revenues are not being included in the model today.** **4 HBF architectural designs from the ecosystem were presented** and we think, while early…"* (Barclays, 2026-08-14). Jefferies goes further than anyone: *"The **HBF update piqued our interest as SNDK made the case that HBF can be used ALONGSIDE HBM but also MAY BE USED IN PLACE OF HBM**. We **wouldn't run out to make a negative HBM call** as there are **tradeoffs in write speeds**, but HBF does fit into a recent theme of **frontier companies exploring different memory tiering given the pricing pressures**"* (Jefferies · Curtis, 2026-08-13). MS takes the opposite side of the substitution question explicitly: *"we would **not expect anything at this point to be zero sum**… **DRAM is a different use case than NAND and carries orders of magnitude higher performance**. What we think is happening is akin to the emergence of SRAM architectures for fast inference; there will be a **continued stratification of AI workloads**"* (2026-08-14). ➜ **The page's standing framing — HBF is free optionality, not a number — is unchanged and now unanimous on the modelling (nobody has it in estimates). What is NEW is that the HBF-displaces-HBM question is now a live sell-side debate rather than a company slide, with Jefferies open to it and MS against. Cross-ref [MU.md](MU.md), [SKHYNIX.md](SKHYNIX.md), [themes/hbm-memory.md](themes/hbm-memory.md).**
- **⚠️ AN INTERNAL INCONSISTENCY IN THE JPM NOTE, FLAGGED NOT ADOPTED:** on edge demand JPM writes *"we expect **unit growth in smartphones and PCs to be >10% in CY26** as demand adjusts to premium mix, **before potentially returning to unit growth in CY27**"* — the two halves of that sentence contradict each other (you cannot return to unit growth from >10% unit growth), and the surrounding logic (demand adjusting to premium mix, price-driven demand destruction elsewhere in the note set) reads as a **>10% unit DECLINE** in CY26. **Logged as printed; the direction is not adopted.** This is the same class of front-page-vs-body drift already documented on this wiki for broker notes.
- **GS's framing of what actually beat, one line:** *"we believe SanDisk's long-term financial guidance… **was well above investor expectations**. Second, management plans to return 100% of excess free cash flow to shareholders, **well ahead of peer announcements to date**"* — while keeping the caveat that acyclicality *"still needs to be proven – and it will take time to be fully reflected in the stock's multiple"* (GS · Schneider, 2026-08-13).

**🆕🔴 2026-08-13 — INVESTOR DAY DELIVERED, AND THE STOCK ROSE ~15-16% ON A DAY THE REST OF THE AI COMPLEX WAS SELLING OFF. THE "PEAK MARGIN" DEBATE THIS PAGE HAS CARRIED SINCE THE AUGUST PRINT WAS ANSWERED WITH A THREE-YEAR ~80% GROSS-MARGIN FRAMEWORK AND A COMMITMENT TO RETURN 100% OF FREE CASH FLOW.**

| FY28–30 long-term model (company) | Target |
|---|---|
| Revenue growth | **mid-to-high teens % y/y** (SIG reads the guide as a **15–20% CAGR**) |
| Gross margin | **~80% average** |
| Operating margin | **75%** |
| FCF margin | **~50%** |
| Capital return | **100% of excess FCF**; **$15.5bn** remaining buyback authorisation |

- **🔴 WHY THE ~80% GM GUIDE IS THE WHOLE EVENT: it is a level, held for three years, that the industry has never sustained.** BofA: *"SNDK laid out a strong financial model for F28-30 with **GM of 80% (much higher than any prior cycle for an extended period of time)**"* and sizes the cash: mid-to-high-teens revenue growth plus a **50% FCF margin** implies **~$100bn of cash flow over the period — "which if used for buybacks could repurchase almost 1/2 of the current market cap"** (BofA · Wamsi Mohan, **Buy, PO $2,500**, px $1,547.99, 2026-08-13). Jefferies' desk read on how it landed: *"the **~80% GPM guide is the standout**, which arguably fell in-line with most expects (which ranged from **70-85%**), **some bears were playing for a midpoint below 80%** — seeing SNDK officially guide for avg 80% margins… should be a **strong assurance for the bull case for sustainable higher level of margins in the industry**"* (Jefferies TMT · Rankie Wong, 2026-08-13, sales & trading commentary, not research).
- **🔴 AND THE TOP LINE IS THE UNDER-DISCUSSED HALF.** Jefferies: *"**don't forget about the top-line guide — avg mid-to-high teens % is also very bullish, and implies big upward revisions to current consensus forecasts (+16% / -4% / -30% y-y for FY28E/29E/30E)**."* ⚠️ **Read that revision profile carefully: consensus is currently modelling FY30 revenue THIRTY PERCENT BELOW the company's own framework. If the guide is credible, the out-year consensus is not merely low, it is modelling a cycle-down that management says will not happen. That is the single largest gap between house guidance and the Street anywhere on this wiki.**
- **🔴 THE NBM (New Business Model) DISCLOSURE, WITH NUMBERS, AND IT IS THE MECHANISM THAT UNDERWRITES THE MARGIN CLAIM:** **8 NBM customers · total contract value $93.9bn · RPO $91.1bn · financial guarantees $16.5bn** (of which ~$2.5bn cash balance, the rest held by or provided through third-party financial institutions). **~50% of FY27 bits and ~2/3 of FY28 bits are already under NBMs**, moving to **50% in 2027 → 67% in 2028** as the predominant model (BofA and SIG, both 2026-08-13). **3 of the 8 NBM customers are US hyperscalers** (Jefferies desk). Management's floor claim: *"**Even at the lowest price, at any point in time, don't expect margins on NBMs to be below 80%**"* (Jefferies desk) — SIG independently: *"**Floor pricing itself supports ~80% GM**, while non-NBM volumes retain upside exposure to market pricing"*, and *"more than **four years of average visibility**"* (SIG · Mehdi Hosseini, **Positive, PT $2,745**, px $1,344.29, 2026-08-13).
- **⚠️ THE PUSHBACK, AND IT IS THE RIGHT ONE: there is no through-cycle model.** *"Some investors pushing back on the **lack of a through-cycle financial model (only a 3-year model given)**"* (Jefferies desk, 2026-08-13). GS makes the same point from the other side: *"although we acknowledge that long-term customer agreements (NBMs) **could help mute industry cyclicality, we think this concept still needs to be proven — and it will take time to be fully reflected in the stock's multiple**"* (GS · James Schneider, **Buy**, 2026-08-13). ➜ **The bull case is now explicitly a multiple-re-rating case contingent on proving acyclicality, not an estimate case.**
- **🔴 SUPPLY OPTIONALITY IS THE MOST INCREMENTAL TECHNICAL DISCLOSURE, AND IT CUTS BOTH WAYS.** SIG: *"SNDK sees **~27% annual bit-per-wafer productivity growth from BiCS5 through BiCS11**, **well above its planned mid-to-high-teens output growth**, providing meaningful headroom should demand surprise to the upside. **The transition from BiCS8 to BiCS10 alone delivers 65% more bits per wafer**."* BofA frames the same roadmap as *"**54% average generation-to-generation bit per wafer growth over the 5 BiCS generations (BiCS5 to BiCS11)**"*. ⚠️ **Note the arithmetic tension between the two houses' framings — ~27% per YEAR (SIG) vs ~54% per GENERATION (BofA) are not the same base and should not be netted; a generation spans more than a year. Both logged as published. The strategic point is common to both: supply growth is now set by the PACE OF TECHNOLOGY TRANSITIONS, not by wafer additions — which is precisely the lever management is choosing to withhold.**
- **The market framing, restated by the company:** **NAND market >$300bn in CY26 (~3x y/y) approaching ~$500bn in CY27** (BofA and SIG, both 2026-08-13). **Data center overtakes consumer and edge as the larger user of flash in 2026** — BofA has **DC at a high-teens CAGR vs Consumer/Edge at 30%+**, while SIG reports **data center at ~50% of industry bits in CY26 vs ~30% last year**. ⚠️ **BofA's two growth rates look transposed relative to the "data center overtakes consumer" claim they accompany — flagged, not adopted; use SIG's bit-share figures as the cleaner statement.**
- **KV cache is now a sized, quantified thesis rather than a narrative:** **~1ZB persistent KV-cache installed base by 2030**, representing **~35% of the NAND market in that year** (SIG); Jefferies desk logs it as *"KVCache to be **35% of the 1.2ZB AI DC TAM by 2030E**."* SIG's net-new architectural point: *"SNDK's proposed **memory hierarchy** places **QLC in the colder, high-capacity data-lake tier, while higher-performance TLC SSDs sit closer to the GPU** and address staging, checkpointing, and hotter KV-cache workloads… **suggests that inference-driven NAND demand may carry a RICHER MIX than a capacity-only thesis would imply**."*
- **⚠️ HBF WAS THE DISAPPOINTMENT, AND THE TWO HOUSES SPLIT ON IT.** Jefferies desk: *"Overall updates on HBF seem **relatively lackluster/disappointing** — clearly seems like it's still relatively far away. **1st HBF product samples in 2027, but no specific timeline for commercial shipments. HBF NOT in current revenue projections.**"* BofA is warmer: *"**SNDK has taped out its first HBF memory die** and expects first HBF inference product samples to be available in 2027"*, with **Meta joining the HBF Technology Consortium** (alongside SNDK, SK Hynix, Google and Tenstorrent). GS calls HBF *"upside optionality."* ➜ **Consistent with the page's existing framing: HBF is free optionality, not a number. The tape-out is the net-new fact; the absence of a commercialisation date is the net-new gap.**
- **PT marks on the day, and note the tension between the tape and the targets:** **BofA Buy, PO $2,500 reiterated, "no change to ests," 10x C27 EPS of $255** (px $1,547.99) · **SIG Positive, PT $2,745 reiterated** (px $1,344.29) · **GS Buy, reiterated** · **Jefferies desk carries Buy, TP $1,750** — consistent with Blayne Curtis's **08-06 cut from $3,000 → $1,750** already logged in the Q1 FY27 full log, i.e. **the Investor Day did NOT reverse the post-print target cut at Jefferies**, even as the desk called the day bullish and the stock +16%. ⚠️ **So the Street's targets straddle the price in both directions after a +15% day: $2,745 and $2,500 above, $1,750 below. The dispersion is the story — nobody moved a number on the day, they moved conviction.**

**🆕 2026-08-10 — FMS/Future-of-Memory-Storage: the ~1ZB KV-cache number, and HBF's first performance data (UBS / Tim Arcuri, 2026-08-10; SIG / Mehdi Hosseini, 2026-08-10).**
- **The headline TAM claim is SanDisk's own and it is enormous.** SNDK estimates **persistent KV-cache capacity requirements reach ~1ZB by C2030** (defined as Direct-Attached SSDs + Network-Attached Compute SSDs + Network-Attached Storage SSDs). UBS's own framing of the magnitude: that would be **>70% of C2027E industry bit supply**. UBS flags the disclosure gap — SNDK did not give concurrent session retention times, cache-miss ratios, input tokens/sec, or KV size per token — but concludes "we struggle to see where all this incremental NAND capacity could come from," given incremental cleanroom capacity prioritises HBM, then DDR, ahead of NAND. **Reads bullish NAND S/D and pricing.**
- **HBF got its first (theoretical) numbers.** In an agentic-AI workload SNDK modelled an **HBM-only config needing 8 GPUs just to fit/run the model, vs 4 HBF-enabled GPUs delivering roughly the same token throughput** (the HBM-only case spilling into slower system DRAM).
