SNDK frames it as **~8x better capital efficiency at the minimum configuration and ~2x GPU efficiency at equivalent token throughput** (UBS, 2026-08-10).
- **The engineering pushback, same week:** Irrational Analysis read the **130-page HBF base-die spec** and softened from "HBF is garbage and will never work" to **"a solution looking for a problem that might work but is not optimal or even good."** Specific flags: the spec **omits UCIe 64G and offers 8G UCIe as an official option** — implying something else in the system (likely the NAND PHY) is the binding limit; **odd clocking requirements** (host must supply a separate REFCLK for the NAND PHYs, spread-spectrum tolerated because the NAND PHY is EMI-sensitive); and **reliability numbers punted to "product specific."** He also names the buy-side motive out loud — "a special subset of hedge fund people who really REALLY want HBF to be a thing just so the NAND flash market gets an epic supply crunch via trade-ratio dynamics." (Irrational Analysis, 2026-08-09.)
- **SIG's preference is the cleaner statement of the bull case:** SNDK is one of two preferred memory names on **"the inflection point in adoption of 2Tb QLC-based NAND/SSD solutions"**, with **332-layer QLC lifting bit density >50%** and mass-capacity SSDs becoming a meaningful incremental 2H26 demand driver accelerating through 2027 (SIG / Hosseini, 2026-08-10).

**Q4 FY26 / June quarter (reported 2026-08-05 AMC — press release + first takes + a detailed call reconstruction from the Jefferies desk; the primary transcript is not on file). Another enormous quarter that MISSED the buy-side on every line, and the debate flipped from "how high" to "have we seen peak margin."** Revenue **$8.97bn, +51% q/q** vs GS $8.84bn and Street **$8.44-8.71bn** — **but vs buy-side bogeys of ~$9.5bn (TMTB) to ~$10bn (Jefferies)**. Non-GAAP gross margin **84.6% (+6.2pp q/q)** vs GS 84.3% and Street 80.0-83.6% — **in line with the ~84% bogey**. Non-GAAP EPS **$39.25** vs GS $38.16 and Street $34.80-35.45 — **but vs bogeys of $42 (TMTB) to $44 (Jefferies)**. Segment shape: **Datacenter +103% q/q, Edge +48% q/q, Consumer −32% q/q.** **Q1 FY27 (Sept) guide: revenue $10.3-10.8bn (midpoint $10.55bn)** vs GS $11.65bn and Street $10.74-11.15bn — ⚠️ **it missed the SELL-SIDE bar too, before you get to bogeys of $11.5bn+ (TMTB) / ~$12bn (Jefferies)**; **gross margin 83-85% (midpoint 84.0%, flattish-to-slightly-down q/q)** vs GS 84.7%; **non-GAAP EPS $44-46 (midpoint $45.00)** vs GS $49.95 and Street $44.26-45.34 — in line with consensus, **against bogeys of $55 (TMTB) / $55-60 (Jefferies)**. **Stock −4% to −6%.** (GS · James Schneider first take, 2026-08-05; Jefferies desk · Rankie Wong "SNDK −6%; Jun-Q first look", 2026-08-05; Barclays desk · Jeffrey Rand, 2026-08-05; TMTB first takes, 2026-08-05.)

⚠️ **The two lines that changed the narrative, and management supplied both.** (1) **The revenue mix behind the quarter: "1/3 of Jun-Q rev growth driven by incremental bits, and 2/3 from pricing — which implies mid-30s% q/q ASP growth vs [[KIOXIA]]'s +70% q/q"** — i.e. SanDisk's realised pricing is running at **half of Kioxia's**, the first hard cross-check on this page of where the price-setter actually sits. (2) **Forward pricing was deliberately downshifted: the Sept guide is "driven by bit growth and MODEST ASP growth" — versus Kioxia's implied Sept-Q ASP guide of +25-30% q/q.** Together with a GM guide of 83-85% against a printed 84.6%, that is management telling the market that the **ASP-escalation leg is flattening**, and it is why the print traded as a peak-margin event rather than a beat. **Jefferies: "bears clearly point to the softer margin guide as a sign of 'peak margins' for SNDK — which is something mgmt IMPLICITLY ADMITTED on the call (mid-80's GPM is a 'FAIR RETURN', and ultimately want to increase visibility and durability of franchise), and ultimately is the trade-off that the companies are making when signing LTAs."** That is the LTA bargain on this page — durability bought with upside — stated by management for the first time.

✅ **The NBM/LTA layer, which is this page's re-rating mechanism, got materially bigger and is the strongest part of the print (Jefferies desk reconstruction of the call, 2026-08-05):**
- **5 additional agreements signed this quarter** (3 with **new** customers, 2 with **existing customers coming back asking for more**) — 3 closed before quarter-end, 2 after. **Total now 8 datacenter and edge customers.**
- **NBM coverage: 50%+ of bits in FY27 (in line with the prior target) and — new disclosure — 2/3 of bits in FY28**, more aggressive than [[KIOXIA]]'s ~50% for CY28.
- **Duration: up to 5 years, weighted-average 4+ years (new disclosure).** Management says it has **"4+ years' of visibility now, with some customers giving demand signals to the end of the decade."**
- **RPO $59.9bn at quarter-end → $91.9bn including the two deals signed after quarter end**; **total expected revenue from all NBM deals a minimum of $93.9bn**, with actual revenue expected above the minimum. **Financial guarantees $16.5bn** (cash deposits + instruments, mostly held via third-party financial institutions), with the **guarantee/RPO ratio rising over time** — exactly the back-end-weighted structure Bernstein's part-2 note argued gives the contracts "real teeth."
- **Pricing has fixed and variable elements with both floors and ceilings — "margins still attractive even at floor pricing (still at ~80% range)," unchanged from last quarter**; and management explicitly told the call **"would not assume NBM are a drag on gross margins."**
- ⚠️ **The bull framing to hold and the one thing that beat expectations: TMTB — "One silver lining is $90B in RPO to date, which is higher than expects of $70B (Bulls will frame this as $90B getting you to $90 of EPS run rate)."**

⚠️ **A genuinely new negative, and it is a supply-side one: FY27 sellable bit growth guided to MID-TEENS % y/y — BELOW the medium-term mid/high-teens framework — because SanDisk is deliberately carrying higher inventory to prepare for the NBMs.** Management: growth comes **primarily through node transitions rather than wafer additions**; capex rises y/y to ramp **BiCS8 and BiCS10** but falls to **~6% of revenue in FY27**. **So the NBM layer that buys durability also costs near-term bits** — the same trade-off, showing up in volume instead of price. **Capital return: an additional share-buyback authorisation announced — Jefferies reports $14bn incremental (vs $6bn announced last quarter), TMTB reports $15bn; ⚠️ the two desks disagree on the figure and the press release is not on file, so both are logged rather than one adopted.**

**Market framing from management (Jefferies call notes, 2026-08-05):** **NAND industry TAM of $300bn+ in CY26 (up ~3x y/y) approaching $500bn in CY27**, with **datacenter share of the TAM going from ~30% in CY25 to 50% in CY26** and continuing to outpace the market in CY27; **bits expected to remain on allocation beyond 2027**; demand "very robust through end-2027 and into 2028." **Investor Day 2026-08-13 (9am ET webcast)** — management teased **HBF timeline/roadmap, KV-cache sizing, and new products/opportunities**, which makes it the near-term catalyst rather than the print.

⚠️ **The buy-side reaction, and the question the desks could not answer: "Initial investor feedback is −ve — this Q was a clear miss to expects, but some are keen to point out that the Sept-Q rev guide actually even missed the SELL-SIDE bar too. Most of the initial questions were around 'WHY ISN'T THE STOCK DOWN MORE?'"** Three cushions offered: the incremental buyback; expectations already lowered after **[[KIOXIA]]'s print flagged consumer weakness**; and **the stock already down ~40% from its June high** (GS makes the same point). ⚠️ **Jefferies' forward framing is the sharpest strategic statement on this page today: "Don't get the sense that buyside EPS estimates of $300+ for 2027E are changing much after this print — which implies a comical <5x P/E at current prices. But probably how cheap the multiple is doesn't matter if the market believes we already reached a cyclical peak… The next leg up for memory stocks from here imho will be driven LESS BY EPS REVISIONS, AND MORE BY MULTIPLE EXPANSION — the bull case is that margins are now at a sustainably higher level vs the peak in any past cycle, the industry has increased in strategic importance… with better visibility/durability, and the companies continue to accelerate buybacks… But this thesis will likely take a few Q's to play out."**

**Prior quarter (Q3 FY26, reported 2026-04-30, quarter ended Apr 3, 2026) for context — a blowout.** Revenue **$5.95B**, +97% q/q, +251% y/y, well past the $4.4–4.8B guide. **Non-GAAP gross margin 78.4%** (vs 51.1% prior q; guide 65–67%); **non-GAAP EPS $23.41** (vs $6.20). Adjusted FCF $2.955B (49.7% margin); cash $3.735B. Crucially, **bits were flat y/y and down high-teens q/q** — the move is entirely **price/mix**, not volume (Q3 FY26 transcript). **Data center $1.467B, +233% q/q** (TLC enterprise SSD + QLC "Stargate" readiness); Edge $3.163B, +118%; Consumer $820M, −10%.

**Business-model shift:** five multiyear supply agreements (NBMs) signed — 3 in Q3, 2 in Q4 — with **$42B minimum contractual revenue** (Q3 contracts) and **>$11B financial guarantees** (incl. ~$400M prepayments), covering **>1/3 of FY27 bit supply** (Goeckeler/Visoso, 2026-04-30). Kioxia JV extended to **Dec-2034**; $1B Nanya investment for DRAM supply. JPM corroborated the LTA layer at its TMC conference and added that SanDisk **extended its undersupply call from end-CY26 to end-CY27**, with the three signed LTAs carrying **floor-and-ceiling pricing and pre-pay-style walkaways with financial guarantees**, and that BiCS 8 (200L+, TLC+QLC) is executing well (JPM "Conf feedback", 2026-05-22). The NBM enforceability mechanics (Bernstein expert, ex-WDC NAND VP Rob, 2026-05-04): the ~$11B guarantee on ~$42B obligations is ~25% — so in a sharp downturn a customer tearing up the deal still leaves SanDisk capturing ~25%, i.e. **a −50% market price would translate to only a ~−25% effective ASP decline** for SanDisk; in practice "tearing up" means renegotiating for more years at a lower price, not walking (Bernstein "HDD/NAND", 2026-05-04).

**Guidance — Q4 FY26:** revenue **$7.75–8.25B**, GM **79–81%**, **EPS $30–33** (158M shares). New **$6B buyback** authorized; remaining $650M term loan repaid; capex held to ~4% of revenue. CY26 data-center growth raised to **mid-70s%** (from 60s%). The trajectory off the spin is steep: Q4 FY25 GM 26.4% / EPS $0.29 (2025-08-14) → Q3 FY26 GM 78.4% / EPS $23.41 in three quarters.

**Stock-price rally arc (May–June 2026):** the tape itself became the story. Barron's "Inside Scoop" (2026-05-18) had SNDK **+465% YTD vs +15% for the Nasdaq 100**, "driven higher by strong fiscal third-quarter earnings and the sustained demand for memory chips" from the AI datacenter buildout, with WDC and Seagate "more than doubled in 2026" and Micron cited as moving in lockstep (Barron's, 2026-05-18). Two weeks later Barron's "Up & Down Wall Street" (2026-06-01) had it at **+523% YTD**, explicitly framing SNDK as a **1990s-tech-boom echo**: "a memory chip maker, [that] got up to $81 in 2000, then plummeted to below $5... In 2016, it was bought by Western Digital, then spun out again just last year" — "ghosts of tech runs past... they're baaaaack" (Barron's, 2026-06-01). The rally is not one-directional: Barron's "The Trader" (2026-06-29) recorded SNDK **tumbling 14% in a single session** (vs the SOX -7.9%) during a chip-stock-led Nasdaq selloff, closing the week at **$2,090.71, down $94.04 (-4.3%) w/w** despite the single-day air-pocket (Barron's, 2026-06-29).

**Enterprise NAND re-accelerated hard in the 30 days into the print — the single most actionable datapoint for the Q4 FY26 guide (Morgan Stanley webcast "2Q26 memory, Intel implications and ARM preview" · Joe Moore, 2026-07-28).** Joe Moore had described the NAND market a month earlier as "**wobbly** a little bit. It wasn't weak but people were looking at the ratio of SSD pricing to hard drive pricing and it was a pretty high ratio. We're slowing down on all that stuff. **Some of the LTAs were seeing some pushback. Q3 price negotiations were tough.**" That has reversed: "**I'm not sure what's happened, but in the last 30 days that's completely changed. Like the NAND market has strengthened materially. I'm seeing prices now above 70 cents, multiple transactions, which would be up 60, 70% from Q2. In enterprise the NAND market has become very, very strong.**" Three reasons this matters for SNDK specifically: (1) **enterprise NAND above 70 cents on multiple transactions is a harder datapoint than a contract-price forecast** and it sits against BofA's F4Q26 preview of +35% q/q ASP growth (Wamsi Mohan, 07-01) and UBS's +30% q/q 3Q26 contract call (Gaudois, 07-03) — the transacted level is running ahead of both; (2) **the LTA pushback that briefly threatened the NBM narrative has reversed**, which is directly on this page's central durability debate (MS's own 06-22 flag that some 2028 enterprise-TLC LTA prices were *down* vs 2027 was the sharpest version of that worry); (3) it is explicitly **enterprise-only** — Joe scopes it "not talking about PCs and phones." The consumer offset is in the Risks section below. Joe's rank order across his coverage places SNDK fourth: **NVDA > AVGO > MU > SNDK**, "in my coverage those are the top four… there's nothing else that's wildly compelling" — so the note is bullish on the NAND tape while ranking SNDK last of four, which is the honest way to hold it. (Morgan Stanley webcast "2Q26 memory, Intel implications and ARM preview" · Joe Moore, 2026-07-28) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_28_2q26_ms_memory_intel_implications_and_arm_preview.html)

## Debate / thesis
- **Bull:** AI is turning NAND structural. The disk transcripts frame NAND as "the only economically viable solution" for trillion-parameter model capacity (Goeckeler, 2026-04-30), with KV-cache alone an estimated **75–100 incremental exabytes in 2027, doubling thereafter** (Q2 FY26, 2026-01-29). The NBM/LTA layer ($42B backlog, >$11B guarantees, >1/3 of FY27 bits locked) is the re-rating mechanism — management argues it makes earnings "durable, structurally higher… less cyclical." Sell-side validation: **MS (Joseph Moore, OW) raised PT to $1,750 from $1,100** on 2026-06-02/03 ("+59% raise… match published estimates"; briefing 2026-06-03). **GS** went "full bullish mode on memory stocks," citing MU + SNDK (briefing 2026-06-01). SNDK is repeatedly named in the GOOG $80B AI-supply-chain map for NAND (ParadisLabs, briefings 2026-06-03). Tape: hit ATHs alongside the memory complex through May (briefings 2026-05-07).
- **Bear:** This is still NAND, and the print is running entirely on price while bits shrink — the classic late-cycle tell. The cross-read from the [HBM/memory theme](themes/hbm-memory.md) and the on-disk Micron ex-VP call (2026-06-10) is that astronomical memory margins normalize hard once new fab capacity lands ~a year out; the AI buildout is a bull-whip several layers from end demand. A second structural worry: the **eSSD-vs-HDD cannibalization that powers the AI-NAND TAM is maturing** — Bernstein argues peak NAND penetration in datacenter storage has likely passed, with the NAND/HDD ASP ($/GB) premium having stabilized at ~6-7x for business-critical HDD (down from 8.8x but no longer compressing), so the easy share-gain leg is mostly behind (Bernstein on HDD, 2025-10-23). FinTwit flagged the crowding: JEF called SNDK the template for "an extremely crowded stock running into the print with high expectations" (briefing 2026-05-07), and Burry/Krinsky's "Party Like It's 1999" analog cited **SNDK +581% as 1999's 2nd-best NDX name** (briefing 2026-05-07) — i.e., a momentum-blowoff comp. Hedgeye's "$25K → $1.06M in 12 months (43x)" is celebrated as a top-signal, not a fundamental (briefings 2026-05-06/07).
  - **"Too promotional," prefer Kioxia (Jefferies, Conor, 2026-04-07):** Jefferies prefers Kioxia *over* SanDisk because SanDisk "is out there telling you everything" (K-B cash ~75-100 EB targets, HBF, etc.) so any disappointment hurts the stock, whereas conservative Kioxia only surprises up. Jefferies also flagged that the SanDisk CEO publicly **dismissing YMTC as "not a real risk" is "really, really stupid"** — YMTC is ~13% NAND share (Q3'25) heading to ~19% by year-end, has the dominant hybrid-bonding/stacking patent pool, and a clear 400-layer roadmap, so it is a genuine NAND-share threat (the China DUV "Match Act" restrictions hit DRAM, not NAND — YMTC has its own X-stacking IP) (Jefferies "Asia tech", 2026-04-07).
  - **HPF risks being "dead on arrival" vs Kioxia Super IO (Redburn, Conor, 2026-06-23):** Conor prefers Kioxia over SanDisk specifically on the latency angle for agentic AI. SanDisk's **HPF** (its ultra-low-latency / high-IOPS answer to Kioxia's **Super IO / XL Flash**, in an R&D project with Global Unichip) **is not due until 2028** — by which point Kioxia's SLC-based Super IO will have been shipping ~2 years (10M units this year vs 3M last, scaling to 100M next, with NVIDIA as partner). His blunt read: **"if Super IO succeeds, HPF is dead on arrival."** (Note: distinct from SNDK's HBF accelerator-attached flash; HPF is the IO/latency product) (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23).
  - **Bernstein: New Memory LTAs deserve a higher multiple — PT to $3,000 (2026-06-29):** Bernstein (Newman) raised SNDK to **PT $3,000 on 11x FY28 EPS of $272 (or 14x through-cycle FY26-30 avg EPS $213; = 7.5x its bull-case FY28 EPS of $400)**, from $1,700 — lifting **base-case FY27/FY28 EPS to $243/$272** (bull $350/$400) on "stronger for longer" pricing peaking June-2027. Relative-to-Street framing: base-case FY27E EPS $243 is **+28% vs consensus**, FY28E EPS $272 is **~27% above Street**, and the bull case ($350/$400) is **84-87% above consensus** (Bernstein · Mark Newman, 2026-06-30 restate). The core argument: the New Memory LTAs (fixed/range-bound prices; upfront financial commitments; 3-5yr terms) structurally dampen cyclicality and justify PE expansion. Bernstein estimates SNDK's **LTA floor price at ~$0.29/GB** (in line with its CQ2'26 ASP), vs Micron's floor ~50% below CQ2'26; SNDK's **>$11B guarantees ≈16% of an estimated ~$69.3bn RPO** for 5 deals (prorated from the disclosed $41.6B for 3 deals) vs MU's $22B ≈22% of ~$100B, though MU's coverage is mostly real cash (~$18B) vs SNDK's ~$0.4B prepaid + the bulk in contingent third-party guarantees. Crucially the guarantee/RPO **coverage ratio rises over time** (≈20% FY27 → 75% FY30) as RPO is fulfilled, so protection is strongest in the back years when it's most needed: in a stress case of market ASP −72% to $0.11 with 60% LTA penetration, **FY30 EPS would still be ~$214** (vs ~$81 with no LTAs) and implied gross margin holds >80%. SNDK average LTA term ~4yrs, more staggered (1/2/3/5yr) vs MU's mostly-5yr book (Bernstein "SNDK New Memory LTAs", 2026-06-29).
  - **Bernstein part 2 — case studies show the LTAs have "real teeth"; PT reiterated $3,000 (2026-07-09):** Newman's follow-up (part 2 of the New Memory LTA series) benchmarks SanDisk's contracts against two *failed* legacy LTA structures — Microchip's **Preferred Supply Program** (12-mo rolling non-cancelable/non-reschedulable backlog, no cash, relaxed to 6mo Aug-2023, discontinued for new orders Feb-2024 — reputational, never enforced) and **Hemlock's polysilicon take-or-pay** (legally bulletproof but uncollectible against insolvent solar buyers) — to argue the New Memory LTAs are categorically stronger on **three structural axes**: (1) **upfront, back-end-weighted financial commitment** (cash deposits / letters of credit / third-party guarantees, with the coverage ratio of remaining collateral to remaining obligations rising toward **75-100% of remaining RPO in the outer years**, exactly when downturn risk is highest — vs Hemlock's static, sue-to-collect terms); (2) **better counterparty quality** (investment-grade, revenue-diversified hyperscalers vs thinly-capitalized/diffuse customer bases); (3) **structural AI demand** (high-end memory sold out through 2026 into 2027) vs Microchip's shortage-driven double-order glut. The load-bearing SNDK figure, restated cleanly: **SanDisk backs an estimated $69B of remaining performance obligations with >$11B of guarantees, most sitting on (escrow-like) third-party balance sheets** — the same ~$69.3bn RPO base modeled in the 06-29 note, and *distinct* from the **$42B minimum contractual revenue** (3 Q3 NBMs) management discloses in Current state (a narrower metric, not a contradiction). Verdict: the LTAs give "**meaningful but not infinite**" downside protection. **PT reiterated Outperform $3,000** (11x FY28 EPS, or 14x through-cycle FY26-30 avg EPS); Bernstein ticker table (8-Jul close **$1,727.18**) now carries **2026E EPS $65.43 / 2027E $243.73 (adj P/E 7.1x)**, TTM adj EPS $2.99. Downside risks unchanged: near-term numbers look high vs cyclical NAND downside, SNDK's confusing disclosures, and NAND weakness that could prove structural (Bernstein "New Memory LTAs part 2", 2026-07-09).
  - **NDR detail — NBM upper bound 70-80%, HBF samples slip to 1H27 (MS, 2026-06-22):** MS hosted SNDK IR head Ivan Donaldson on a European NDR. Net-new: management sees **70-80% of bits as the practical NBM upper bound** (data center could be largely covered; consumer/auto/industrial less so, and not all customers have the balance sheet); NBM margins are **similar to the current ~80% level at the floor ASPs**, and signing them is not viewed as an ASP-increase headwind (supply assurance for demand assurance, not trading margin for visibility); penalties are **intentionally back-end weighted** (~$11B), CFO/board-approved, and some customers have **already asked to add to existing deals**. In a downcycle, SNDK would proactively cut output (taking underutilization charges) but collect guarantee cash to avoid fire-selling inventory. **HBF: samples now expected 1H2027 with customer feedback by mid-2027** (vs the prior "first samples 2H26" framing), uses SLC with an order-of-magnitude more bandwidth than peers' SLC-in-PCIe approaches, and the opportunity "would need to be measured in tens of billions" to justify the resource allocation; SK Hynix remains the sole standardization partner (expanding it could be counterproductive). SNDK still plans around **mid-to-high-teens bit growth** and is **not currently planning US fabs** (Japan scale + incentives a high hurdle). MS stays OW but keeps a slight preference for Micron (lower multiple; DRAM tighter) (MS "SanDisk NDR", 2026-06-22).
  - **NAND a notch below DRAM; some 2028 LTA pricing *down* (MS, 2026-06-22):** MS rates NAND fundamentals "8 out of 10" vs DRAM's "10/10," and — while SanDisk has become "everyone's preferred investment" and is consistently the price-setter ("SanDisk is being the bad guy," with Koreans pricing just below) — MS still prefers Micron because **SanDisk looks more expensive on 2028 numbers** after its big move. The caution: some NAND **2028 LTA lock-in prices are *down* from 2027, in some cases "materially so"** (esp. enterprise-grade TLC), though built-in **price escalators** mean the realized price rises if the market is up. Q3 NAND pricing seen up ~20-30% QoQ (MS "Memory & semicap", 2026-06-22).
  - **BofA: stronger-for-longer pricing cycle through mid-2027 — raises FY27/28 EPS +24%/+14.5%, new PO $2,500 (Wamsi Mohan, 2026-07-01):** BofA raised FY2027 EPS **+24% to $233.07** and FY2028 EPS **+14.5% to $254.24**, arguing the pricing up-cycle runs "stronger for longer" through mid-2027; F4Q26 (June quarter) preview looks for **+13% QoQ bit growth and +35% QoQ ASP growth**, with supply/demand imbalance persisting. New **PO $2,500**. Roughly **1/3 of 2027 revenue is currently under New Business Model (NBM) long-term contracts**, with that share expected to keep rising — offering visibility past FY28 (BofA · Wamsi Mohan, 2026-07-01). The desk (Brian Fenske) flagged the **40-50% upward FY28 EPS revision across SNDK/STX/WDC** in Mohan's note as an unusually large, "major" one-day estimate raise across the group (BofA sales · Brian Fenske, 2026-07-01).
  - **Jefferies: SNDK the best-performing >$5bn stock in 1H26, but a top contributor now derating on rotation (Jeffrey Favuzza, 2026-06-30/07-01):** Jefferies flagged SNDK as the **best-performing stock with a market cap >$5bn in 1H26, up ~765%** (Jefferies · Jeffrey Favuzza, 2026-06-30). The next day, despite the bullish EPS revisions from BofA, Jefferies Tech noted SNDK as the **#6 Nasdaq 100 YTD contributor (5%) and #6 S&P 500 contributor (5%)**, yet trading **pre-market -3.8% on 2026-07-01 (~$539mn notional)** — part of a broader leader/laggard rotation that day (Jefferies Tech · Jeffrey Favuzza, 2026-07-01).
  - **MS: OW reiterated on SanDisk + Micron; Fadu the key eSSD-controller supplier (MS, Joseph Moore / Ryan Kim, 2026-07-02):** MS keeps **OW on SanDisk (and MU)** as "supply-demand outcomes continue to look compelling for both NAND and DRAM"; the biggest risk is a broad AI-capex slowdown, which MS doesn't expect near-term — supporting **higher earnings power well beyond 2027** (vs consensus assuming pricing normalizes then). At **<10x P/E for both SNDK and MU**, MS sees room for multiples to benefit from extended (LTA-driven) visibility, with a market that "could/should stay tight for another 2-3 years," and expects **SanDisk and Micron to remain attractive capital-return stories with meaningful buybacks in 2027**. MS order-of-preference PT: **$1,750** (bull $2,635 / bear $1,100). Tactically MS **prefers DRAM over NAND** (better LTA terms, higher demand visibility, EUV-capped supply, potential HBM4E squeeze) and within NAND **prefers suppliers over module makers** on margin durability — but SanDisk stays OW. **Net-new supply-chain angle:** MS names **Fadu (440110.KQ, OW) as "a key eSSD controller supplier to SanDisk"** — MS raised its SIMO PT (US$155→US$400) to reflect the eSSD-controller opportunity, and flags Fadu as "transitioning from a recovery story to a structurally scaling AI-storage semiconductor story," with 2026 revenue of ~W300bn+ "effectively secured" via hyperscaler enterprise-SSD controllers, and the potential to cover 3-4 of the top global hyperscalers — an under-tracked read-through into SanDisk's eSSD supply chain (Morgan Stanley "NAND Industry Outlook", 2026-07-02). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Nand_-_MS.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Nand_-_MS.html)
- **Goldman Sachs Buy, PT to $2,200 from $1,200 — CY26 EPS >~30% above Street (James Schneider, 2026-07-05):** GS reiterates **Buy** and **raises its 12-mo PT to $2,200 (from $1,200)**, on **20x (down from 22x on a higher base of growth) applied to normalized EPS of $110 (up from $55 on significantly higher estimates)**. GS expects a **very strong quarter and guidance** driven by continued NAND supply tightness; its **CY26 non-GAAP EPS estimate is over ~30% above Street** (raised on updated NAND contract-pricing assumptions + higher opex). GS argues **NAND tightness will persist, potentially longer than in DRAM**, given limited supply additions, and sees improving product mix from **ramping eSSD design wins at key hyperscaler customers**. LTAs a key focal point for investors post MU's recent agreements. Key downside risks: structural NAND-pricing change fails to materialize; YMTC roadmap iteration; SNDK fails to gain eSSD traction (Goldman Sachs "Semiconductors 2Q Preview", 2026-07-05).

(UBS's DDR/DRAM contract forecast was raised in parallel to +32% QoQ 3Q26 / +18% QoQ 4Q26, with the DRAM industry undersupplied until at least 2Q28.) UBS remains positive on the memory group, seeing the industry generate close to US$1.2tn of FCF in 2027E, with the main risk being customer (esp. hyperscaler) affordability. A direct supply-side corroboration of the SNDK undersupply/pricing thesis — though note UBS's NAND-vendor Buys are the Asian names (SK Hynix, Kioxia, Samsung), not SNDK directly (UBS "Memory Semis Monthly", 2026-07-03).

Two cuts, four reiterations, ZERO downgrades.** ⚠️ **Jefferies (Blayne Curtis) $3,000 → $1,750, a −42% cut that takes the joint-highest target on the page down to the LOWEST, while keeping the BUY. Susquehanna (Mehdi Hosseini) $3,050 → $2,745 (−10%), Positive kept.** Reiterated unchanged: **BofA (Wamsi Mohan) Buy PO $2,500** (10x C27 EPS $255), **Goldman Sachs (James Schneider) Buy PT $2,200**, **MS (Joseph Moore) Overweight PT $1,750** ("our conviction remains high"), **Barclays (Tom O'Malley)** constructive ("attractive on a pullback"). Not re-marked in this flow and therefore **STALE: Bernstein $3,000 (Newman), Barclays research $2,300, Arete $2,300, Mizuho $2,200.** **The revised ladder as marked: $2,745 Susquehanna → $2,500 BofA → $2,200 GS → $1,750 Jefferies = MS.** ⚠️ **The shape of the revision is the signal, not the level: Jefferies RAISED its near quarters (Q3 EPS $46.07 from $44.60, Cal-26 $160.29 from $154.60) and CUT Cal-2027 revenue/EPS by −14% each, moving from above consensus to −12%/−10% BELOW it. The 42% haircut is therefore entirely 2027-durability and multiple, not near-term earnings — the same distinction the page has been tracking since June, now expressed as a price target. And the diagnosis of the miss is unanimous across all five houses and it is BITS, NOT PRICE: volume is being deliberately sacrificed (higher DOI, mid-teens FY27 sellable-bit growth) to fund the NBM inventory. The durability trade is now visibly being paid for.**

Price ref **$1,528.11** (13-Aug close, post the +15–16% day). ⚠️ **Two things to hold: (1) the Investor Day produced exactly ONE rating action and ZERO PT changes among houses already covering — every existing target was reiterated, which means the +15% move was entirely multiple, entirely delivered by investors, and unvalidated by any analyst re-mark; (2) the $500 gap between the $1,750 pair and the $2,200+ cluster is the SAME gap as before the day, and it maps precisely onto the MS-vs-JPM fault line: MS/Jefferies underwrite a long shortage, the $2,200+ houses underwrite acyclicality.** **Stale and not re-marked in this flow: Bernstein $3,000 (Newman), Arete $2,300, Mizuho $2,200.** 🆕 **UPDATE 2026-08-17: one of the three stale entries is now re-marked, and DOWN — Mizuho (Vijay Rakesh) cut to $1,900 from $2,200 on 2026-08-16, Outperform maintained (px $1,641.11).** That places Mizuho inside the previously EMPTY $1,750-$2,100 band, so this block's "still nobody in between" observation no longer holds strictly. ⚠️ **Qualifier: the cut came in an AI-server industry note with no SNDK-specific model work, and it followed a ~14% fall in the share price — a lower PT on a lower price is not automatically a lower multiple. Bernstein $3,000 and Arete $2,300 remain stale.**
- 🆕 **A HISTORICAL MARK ADDED TO THE LADDER FOR COMPLETENESS, EXPLICITLY *BELOW* AND *BEHIND* EVERYTHING ABOVE — AND THE REASON IT IS WORTH THE LINE IS THE FRAMEWORK, NOT THE NUMBER (China Renaissance · Jack Zhou, "AI 101 Series: AI inference is reshaping the future of memory", 2026-04-30, 107pp; ingested 2026-08-21).** ⚠️⚠️ **DATED 2026-04-30 — ~3.7 MONTHS STALE. Its mark: BUY, TP US$1,452, raised from US$698 (+108%), on 8x FY27E (end-Jun-2027) P/E — "the valuation level in past cycle peaks" — against a 04/28/26 reference price of US$1,002.35 (+45% implied). Its FY26E/27E/28E EPS were US$52.48 / US$181.50 / US$191.31.** 🔴 **THIS IS RECORDED AS A HISTORICAL/SUPERSEDED MARK AND NOTHING ON THIS PAGE MOVES BECAUSE OF IT. It sits BELOW every live target here — SIG $2,745, BofA $2,500, Barclays $2,300, JPM $2,250, GS $2,200, Citi $2,100, MS $1,750, Jefferies $1,750, and Bernstein $3,000 — and it is 3.7 months older than all of them. Under the thesis-drift rule the Changelog is for values a NEW note supersedes; an OLDER note cannot push newer numbers into it, so no current PT, rating or estimate on this page was touched, moved or archived on the strength of this note. Its April EPS series is likewise vintage, NOT an estimate: the Q4 FY26 print (08-05) and the 08-13 Investor Day are both after it.** ➡ **WHAT *IS* DURABLE, AND IT IS GOOD: the note's framework, which is the cleanest third-party statement on this page of why HBF is a TIER rather than a faster SSD. (1) AI inference creates a NEW DATA-ACCESS LAYER *BETWEEN* HBM/DRAM AND TRADITIONAL STORAGE, "fundamentally reshaping the memory hierarchy." (2) The workloads — RAG, vector search, KV-cache expansion, agentic reasoning — generate "small, highly concurrent, RANDOM READS," so the binding constraint becomes IOPS, latency and concurrency rather than bandwidth or TB/TCO; the industry metric moves "from TB/TCO to IOPS/TCO." (3) Flash therefore transitions from CAPACITY-DRIVEN to PERFORMANCE-DRIVEN. (4) The commercial consequence: HIGH-IOPS FLASH AND HBF "CAN DELIVER 5-10x ASP UPLIFT AND HIGHER MARGINS," with value redistributing to "performance enablers" — controllers, performance-tier NAND and AI-optimised SSD vendors — while capacity-only segments face "increasing commoditization."** **On SanDisk specifically it is the HBF-first case, and it lands on the SAME architecture claim this page carries from six other houses: HBF built on BiCS 3D NAND with CBA wafer bonding, "near-HBM bandwidth with 8-16x higher capacity at comparable cost," positioned as a COMPLEMENTARY tier rather than an HBM replacement, matching HBM form factors "for seamless integration," and offloading model weights, KV-cache extensions and large retrieval datasets. Its stated system-level specs: up to 1.6TB/s read bandwidth, higher capacity per stack (e.g. 16-die stacking via advanced packaging), and lower cost per bit with no refresh power because it is non-volatile.** ⚠️ **AND ONE PIECE OF THIS NOTE IS USEFUL PRECISELY BECAUSE IT IS STALE — IT DATES THE HBF SLIP FROM THE OUTSIDE. In April it recorded SanDisk's OWN official guidance as "HBF samples in 2HCY26 and commercialization in early-2027." This page now carries samples in 2027 with NO commercial shipment timeline and HBF explicitly outside the FY28-30 model (Investor Day, 08-13), after MS logged the slip to 1H27 on 06-22. So the April note is a clean third-party timestamp on where the goalposts started — the sampling date has moved roughly two quarters to the right in four months. Treat its "2HCY26 sampling" as HISTORY, never as an expectation.** ⚠️ **It also ranks SanDisk SECOND, behind [[KIOXIA]] as sector Top Pick ("pecking order: KIOXIA > Sandisk > Macronix"), on the grounds that Kioxia leads high-IOPS NAND MANUFACTURING while Sandisk is "the pioneer in high-IOPS and HBF solutions" — the same Kioxia-over-SanDisk preference Jefferies (04-07) and Redburn (06-23) express on this page, reached independently and from an architecture argument rather than a latency-vs-die-penalty one.** ⚠️ **Pageless names in the same note, plain text only: Phison (`8299 TT`) TP TWD2,540 and FADU (`440110 KS`) TP KRW103,800, both initiated at BUY as eSSD controller/solution providers; Macronix (`2337 TT`) TP TWD251.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260510_China_Renaissance_Research_SNDK_Multi_Company_Report_AI_101_Series-_AI_inference_is_re.html)
- **🆕 THE FAULT LINE, STATED AS A TESTABLE PROPOSITION (2026-08-14).** JPM: the NBM framework *"has **structurally reset SNDK's margin profile higher and materially reduced cyclicality**"* and *"screens as a **substantive reset rather than peak-cycle pricing capture**."* MS, from an equally-rated OW: *"we **aren't completely in agreement** with management's view that improvements are **more structural than cyclical**… we **don't see 75%+ operating margins being sustainable; that's happening because there is a shortage**."* ➜ **Both are long. They fail at different moments: MS's thesis breaks when NAND stops being short; JPM's breaks only if the contracts break. The first NAND price rollover is the experiment that separates them, and it is the single highest-value thing to watch on this page.**

- **Where the sell-side/buy-side stood before (pre-08-06, kept for the drift record):** Bullish and getting more so. **MS OW PT $1,750** (2026-06-03), **GS bullish** (2026-06-01); SNDK structurally featured in hyperscaler-capex supply maps. On-disk PT ladder now: **Jefferies $3,000** (Blayne Curtis, 06-27, C27/C28 EPS $238/$248), **Bernstein $3,000** (Mark Newman, 06-29, 11x FY28 EPS $272, base FY27/28 EPS $243/$272), **BofA $2,500** (Wamsi Mohan, 07-01, new PO), **Barclays $2,300** (upgraded to OW from EW, 10x CY27 EPS $230.10 — with an 8x "proxy floor" of $1,320, ~17% downside; 2026-05-26), **Goldman Sachs $2,200** (Buy, Schneider, 07-05, 20x normalized EPS $110; CY26 EPS >~30% above Street), **Mizuho $2,200** (OW, Vijay Rakesh, 07-11 — raised from $1,825 05-27, see Changelog), **MS $1,750** — a wide dispersion reflecting the 2028-durability debate. **Arete (Nam Hyung Kim) Buy, PT $2,300 (2026-07-21)** — headlines the **HBF IP** (SanDisk + SK Hynix standardising; 512GB 16-die → 1.5TB gen-3) as a promising long-term HBM complement, with the SK Hynix M17 / Samsung P5 NAND greenfields creating the die headroom (see the 07-21 Intra-quarter row + [hbm-memory theme](themes/hbm-memory.md)). **Note:** Jefferies' $3,000 and Bernstein's $3,000 are two *different banks'* PTs that happen to land on the same round number one business day apart (06-27 vs 06-29) — Jefferies models C27/C28 EPS $238/$248, Bernstein models base-case FY27/FY28 EPS $243/$272 (bull $350/$400); do not read one as a revision of the other. BofA's $2,500 PO (07-01) is a third, distinct new target, issued alongside a large EPS estimate raise (see Catalysts and Intra-quarter log). The debate, as with MU, is no longer direction but **duration** — bulls underwrite multi-year tightness + LTA-locked bits; bears see a price-only blowoff in a commodity that always mean-reverts. No bear-side broker PT on disk; the bear case is voiced via FinTwit and the memory-theme read-through.
- **How the call evolved (early-coverage record):** worth anchoring how fast the Street re-rated — the original bull thesis pre-dates the price spike. **MS (Joe Moore) elevated SNDK to Top Pick, PT $96 (from $70), bull $150 / bear $40** (MS AI NAND overview, 2025-09-10) — base 12x through-cycle EPS $8; bull 15x through-cycle EPS $10 (50%+ GM precedent in prior peaks); bear 8x through-cycle EPS $5, ~book value. The MS framing then was eSSD-driven, not LTA-driven: hyperscalers placing "10s of exabytes" of eSSD orders into a ~250-300EB CY25 market, SNDK raising channel/consumer prices ~10%, BiCS 8 as the eSSD share catalyst. **Bernstein (Mark Newman) initiated OW, PT $120** (Bernstein initiation, 2025-09-15) on a deep-value / replacement-cost case — fab replacement value ~$25B, stock at a 54% discount; valued 10x CY27 EPS (≈13.4x est. $1.3B FY27 FCF); explicit M&A optionality (Micron — whose CEO Mehrotra co-founded SanDisk — and SK Hynix as potential acquirers, cheaper than greenfield in a down-cycle); HBF treated as unmodeled call-option upside. Both PTs ($96 / $120) are now far below the $1,750 / Q3-FY26 print and are superseded — but the structural eSSD / HBF / replacement-cost logic is the same thesis that later re-rated on the LTA layer.
- **🆕 ARCHIVE BACKFILL — **DAMNANG (Substack)** — independent Korean-semis Substack, launched 2026-03-15, ~9k subscribers, PAID; also publishes as **Damnang2**. ⚠️ NOT A BROKER: no rating, no price target, no house model. 3-5 MONTHS OLD; HISTORICAL FRAMEWORK ONLY, and NONE of it supersedes any current number, rating or thesis above — in particular the FY26 Q2 financials in the first piece are LONG SUPERSEDED by the Q4 FY26 print and the Investor Day and are deliberately NOT reproduced here.**
  - **The single most SNDK-specific outside piece the wiki holds — a forensic audit of the March-2026 20%-in-a-week drawdown, and every one of its four structural conclusions is still live** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — “Is SanDisk Done?”, **2026-03-26**; written as the stock went $772 → $623, ~$20bn of market cap in five sessions). ① **THE NANYA DEAL, CORRECTLY SIZED — AND THE MARKET GOT IT WRONG BECAUSE IT LOOKED AT SNDK ALONE: SanDisk's $1bn / 3.9% stake was part of a SINGLE ~$2.5bn (NT$78.7bn) private placement at NT$223.9 that ALSO included Solidigm ([[SKHYNIX]]) at $500M / 2%, [[KIOXIA]] at $500M / 2% and CISCO at $500M / 2% — with all three SSD makers signing SEPARATE multi-year DRAM supply agreements and a 3-year lock-up under Taiwanese law.** The author's read: *“three SSD manufacturers locking in DRAM sourcing at the same time sends a strong signal: THE ENTIRE INDUSTRY RECOGNIZES A DRAM BOTTLENECK.”* The engineering reason is specific and durable: **enterprise SSD controllers hold the FTL mapping table in DRAM, so constrained DRAM is a direct bottleneck on high-performance eSSD output** — and Nanya matters because the Big 3 have deprioritised DDR4 to the point that **TrendForce spot showed DDR4/16G trading ABOVE DDR5/16G**. ⚠️ **He flags the second rationale as HYPOTHESIS, not fact: a DRAM relationship could be groundwork for HBF, “SanDisk hasn't officially drawn this connection.”** ② **THE LAYER-COUNT GAP, AND THE OPTIMISTIC WAY TO READ IT: as of March 2026, SK hynix at 321L QLC, Samsung V9 (~290L class) TLC, [[MU]] 276L G9 QLC, SanDisk/Kioxia BiCS8 at 218-232L with BiCS9 (300L class) targeted 2026 and BiCS10 (332L) originally 2H27 (Nikkei-reported pull-in). *“It's true that SanDisk/Kioxia TRAIL SK hynix by roughly a year on layer count… But flip it around: the 218-layer to 300+ layer transition gives them SIGNIFICANT ROOM to increase bit density per die… SK hynix at 321 layers is APPROACHING THE LIMITS of how much additional efficiency it can extract, while SanDisk/Kioxia STILL HAVE STEPS LEFT TO CLIMB.”* ➤ **This is the cleanest available articulation of why the page's ‘growth via node transitions, not wafer additions’ guide is credible rather than a constraint.** ③ **CBA IS THE STRUCTURAL ASSET AND IT IS UNDER-DISCUSSED HERE: CMOS-directly-Bonded-to-Array fabricates array and logic on separate wafers and hybrid-bonds them, enabling high-speed interfaces such as Toggle DDR 6.0 (4.8 GT/s). YMTC commercialised it first as Xtacking; SanDisk/Kioxia adopted it from BiCS8. “It directly enables eSSD performance differentiation and… serves as A TECHNICAL PREREQUISITE FOR HBF.”** ④ **THE TURBOQUANT EPISODE — kept because the same category error will recur: Google's KV-cache compression (PolarQuant + QJL; ~6x KV-cache memory reduction at 3-bit with no accuracy loss; separately up to 8x attention speed-up at 4-bit on H100 — ⚠️ the author is explicit that the memory saving and the speed-up are DIFFERENT configurations, not simultaneous) compresses KV CACHE, which lives in HBM/system DRAM. “What SanDisk sells is SSDs. What's stored on SSDs is NOT KV cache. It's MODEL WEIGHTS, TRAINING DATA, CHECKPOINTS, INFERENCE DATASETS… the portion of SanDisk's decline attributable to TurboQuant is most likely the result of THE MARKET FAILING TO DISTINGUISH BETWEEN DRAM AND NAND and selling ‘memory’ as a single bucket.”** ⚠️ **His own caveat carried: second-order effects (GPU efficiency → DC architecture → storage configuration) are “still playing out and worth monitoring,” and he leans on Jevons rather than proving it.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026-03-26_-_Is_SanDisk_Done-.html)
  - **HBF, dismantled from SK hynix's own researchers' paper — the most rigorous public scepticism on the technology this page treats as optionality** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — “HBF's Limits and Reality: Between Promise and Structural Obstacles (H³ Paper Review)”, **2026-03-18**). The promise: **stack NAND with TSVs like HBM for ~16x HBM capacity (~3TB) at equivalent ~8TB/s bandwidth at ~1/5 the cost of HBM**, SanDisk and SK hynix targeting **sampling in late 2026**. The paper itself is a HYBRID (H³ = HBM + HBF) because the objection is physical: **NAND's 1-2 ORDER-OF-MAGNITUDE latency disadvantage cannot be architected away**, and the mitigation — **a 40MB SRAM buffer plus tens of GB of DRAM plus a complex FTL controller** — *“makes the total system ANYTHING BUT CHEAP.”* Second objection: the paper **assumes pure READ-ONLY workloads and deterministic access patterns**, “extremely limited in actual production.” Open: production yield, thermal management in a GPU environment, read-disturb/endurance under those conditions. **His verdict: “A NICHE SOLUTION effective in highly specialized CAG workloads… A COMPLEMENT targeting ‘the gap between HBM and SSD,’ NOT AN HBM REPLACEMENT” — while being explicit that “SanDisk and SK hynix's technical capabilities are BEYOND QUESTION… they'll prove this technology works at the 2026-2027 sample stage. But ‘it works’ and ‘it succeeds’ are different questions.”** ➤ **Independently corroborated five months later from the commercial side by MICRON'S OWN MANAGEMENT (Mizuho, 2026-08-09): substitution “unlikely… but will see adoption in the memory stack”, with HBF at ~$1/GB vs HBM4e ~$30/GB. TWO INDEPENDENT SOURCES, ONE PHYSICAL AND ONE COMMERCIAL, SAME DESTINATION — which is the reason to price HBF as a TIER with a TAM, not as a call option on HBM's TAM.** The strategic framing is worth keeping regardless of the product: *“SK hynix dominates DRAM and HBM, while SanDisk dominates NAND… an example of the memory industry's effort to transition from COMMODITY business to PLATFORM business.”* [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026-03-19_-_HBFs_Limits_and_Reality-_Between_Promise_and_Structural_Obstacles_H%C2%B3_Paper_Review.html)
  - **Why storage gets sold on DRAM news, and why that is usually wrong** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — “Vera Rubin's DRAM Cut in Half: Is the Memory Cycle Really Over?”, **2026-06-08** — ⚠️ **the ingest router mislabelled this piece “SemiAnalysis”; it is DAMNANG, DISCUSSING a SemiAnalysis report. Verified in the text**). On the June selloff that took SanDisk down with Micron on the NVIDIA SOCAMM cut: *“bundling storage into the same bad news does not quite add up. If the core of the report is that THE PLATFORM OWNER ECONOMIZES ON EXPENSIVE MEMORY AND PUSHES LESS HOT STATE TO AN OUTER TIER, then where that state ends up settling is STORAGE in the end. BY DIRECTION ALONE, THIS IS NOT A BAD STORY FOR STORAGE. Yet in the market… HBM, DRAM, NAND, and storage get treated as ONE BUNCH as long as there is memory exposure… That is also why PURE STORAGE COMPANIES GET THE SAME TREATMENT.”* ⚠️ **His own discipline, carried: “you cannot conclude straight away that this benefits NAND. The demand moving over to storage is not one kind. There is demand ALREADY CONFIRMED IN RESULTS, and demand that is STILL ONLY IN THE BLUEPRINTS of NVIDIA and the server makers.”** ➤ **The taxonomy — state that is CAPTIVE in HBM (hot KV, intermediate), ABSORBED into the DRAM family (LPDDR/SOCAMM), or LEAKED to storage — is the single most useful frame available for the August-2026 HBM de-spec argument, and it says a content cut at the GPU is directionally GOOD for the outer tier. Confirmatory evidence he cites from the vendors' own 1Q results: [[SAMSUNG]] referenced PCIe Gen6 eSSD and KV-cache storage demand; [[SKHYNIX]] said AI is growing NAND demand alongside DRAM; [[KIOXIA]] tied inference-era storage to GPU extension.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026-06-08_-_Vera_Rubins_DRAM_Cut_in_Half-_Is_the_Memory_Cycle_Really_Over-.html)


## Catalysts / what to watch
- ~~**Q4 FY26 print** (fiscal year-end June; reported ~Aug-2026) — vs guide rev $7.75–8.25B / GM 79–81% / EPS $30–33.~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-08-05**: rev **$8.97bn** / GM **84.6%** / EPS **$39.25**, beating its own guide on all three (revenue above the $8.25B high end, GM ~360bps above the 81% high end, EPS above the $33 high end) **and beating the Street on all three — but missing buy-side bogeys on all three.** Sept guided **$10.55bn mid / GM 84.0% / EPS $45.00** — ⚠️ **the revenue guide missed the SELL-SIDE too** ($10.74-11.15bn). **Stock −4% to −6%.** BofA's preview (+13% q/q bits, +35% q/q ASP) was directionally right on the ASP but the mix is now disclosed: **1/3 of growth from bits, 2/3 from pricing → ~mid-30s% q/q ASP**. See Current state.
- ~~**🆕 SanDisk INVESTOR DAY — 2026-08-13**~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-08-13**: FY28–30 model given (revenue mid-to-high teens growth, **GM ~80%**, **OM 75%**, **FCF margin ~50%**), **100% of excess FCF returned**, NBM detail ($93.9bn TCV / $91.1bn RPO / 8 customers / $16.5bn guarantees), **~1ZB KV-cache and ~35% of the 2030 NAND market**, HBF first die taped out with samples in 2027 and **no commercial timeline**. **Stock +15–16%.** See `## Current state`. ➜ **The catalyst now rolls forward into three follow-ons: (1) whether a THROUGH-CYCLE model ever gets published — the loudest investor pushback on the day; (2) an HBF commercialisation date; (3) whether FY29/FY30 consensus closes the -4%/-30% gap to the company framework (Jefferies desk, 2026-08-13).**
- **🆕 SanDisk INVESTOR DAY — 2026-08-13, 9am ET webcast (original catalyst entry, retained for the audit trail).** Management explicitly teased **HBF timeline/roadmap, KV-cache sizing, and other new products/opportunities** (Jefferies, 2026-08-05). This page carries HBF as un-modelled optionality (Bernstein) with samples having already slipped once — **1H27 samples / mid-27 customer feedback** (MS NDR, 06-22) from the prior "2H26 samples" framing — so the single most valuable thing the day can produce is a **dated** HBF roadmap. KV-cache sizing is the other one: this page's standing number is **75-100 incremental exabytes in 2027, doubling thereafter** (Q2 FY26 call), which has never been updated by management.
- **🆕 "Peak margin" is now the governing debate, and management put the words on the record.** Mid-80s GPM described as a **"fair return"**, Sept GM guided **flat-to-down** at 83-85%, and forward revenue growth attributed to **bits + "modest" ASP growth**. Watch the Dec-Q guide for whether GM holds the mid-80s — that is the single number that adjudicates Jefferies' "multiple expansion, not EPS revisions" thesis against the peak-cycle bears. Related tell: **[[KIOXIA]] is guiding Sept ASP +25-30% q/q against SanDisk's "modest"** — if that gap persists it is either share loss or price discipline, and the two have opposite implications.
- **🆕 FY27 sellable bit growth cut to mid-teens % y/y (from mid/high-teens) on deliberate NBM inventory build.** The volume cost of the durability trade. Watch whether it reverses in FY28 as the NBM book matures, and whether **BiCS8/BiCS10 node transitions** (capex up in dollars, down to ~6% of revenue) can carry mid/high-teens without wafer additions.
- **NBM / LTA cadence** — incremental multiyear agreements and how much of FY27/FY28 bits get locked (and at fixed vs variable pricing) is the durability catalyst.
- **BiCS 8 ramp** — to majority of bit production exiting FY26 (Q1 FY26, 2025-11-06); QLC "Stargate" hyperscaler revenue ramp. BiCS 8 + controller investment is also the lever to close SNDK's eSSD share gap vs Micron / Hynix-Solidigm (MS AI NAND overview, 2025-09-10). The Bernstein expert (ex-WDC NAND VP, 2026-05-04) flagged that essentially **all high-capacity AI drives (128/256TB) are QLC**, a "very hard" technology where SanDisk + Kioxia "have done quite well" (Samsung struggling, Micron early lead then questions) — so QLC mix is both the growth lever and a real differentiator on the latest node.
- **High Bandwidth Flash (HBF)** — the bull-case optionality on AI accelerator-attached flash; introduced at the Feb-2025 Investor Day, "Best of Show" at FMS. HBF stacks 3D-NAND with TSVs on a base interposer (HBM-like) to give the GPU large NAND capacity on-package, targeting AI inference: **8-16x the capacity of HBM at similar bandwidth (~1.2TB/s)**, ~288-576GB per stack vs ~36GB for HBM3E, enabling up to 4TB of VRAM on a GPU (gen 1). **SK Hynix signed an MOU with SanDisk (Aug-2025)** to standardize the spec and build an ecosystem. **First HBF samples 2H26; first AI-inference devices with HBF early 2027.** Caveat: not "drop-in" compatible with HBM (minor protocol changes, requires GPU-level redesign), and CSP/fabless adoption is still early — watch for design-win commentary from fabless accelerator players first (MS AI NAND overview, 2025-09-10). Bernstein flags HBF as large but un-diligence-able upside, deliberately not in its numbers (Bernstein initiation, 2025-09-15). The ex-WDC NAND VP corroborated HBF as "a direct drop-in replacement for HBM" exploiting NAND's fast *streaming* read for AI-inference coefficient loading — "very promising" but "still a bit early," needing power/performance/reliability proof; he also sees broader AI demand for performance/capacity NAND (KV-cache, high-perf cache tiers à la VAST) (Bernstein "HDD/NAND", 2026-05-04).
- **WDC stake unwind** — UBS (Arcuri) tracked WDC exchanging SNDK shares for WDC stock toward full redemption by Sep-Q (briefings 2026-05-06/07); residual overhang/technical.
- **$6B buyback** execution against the elevated multiple.
- **M&A optionality** — Bernstein flagged SNDK as a plausible target (Micron/SK Hynix) at the pre-spike discount-to-replacement valuation (Bernstein initiation, 2025-09-15); largely moot at the current multiple but still a structural feature of a sub-scale NAND pure-play.

## Risks
- **Commodity cyclicality** — NAND ASPs remain volatile; current results are price-driven with declining bits, the canonical setup for a sharp reversal when capacity normalizes (theme read-through; Micron ex-VP, 2026-06-10).
- **The NAND market has split, and the weak half is the bigger half by bits (Morgan Stanley webcast "2Q26 memory, Intel implications and ARM preview" · Sean, 2026-07-28).** The same webcast where Joe Moore reports enterprise NAND above 70 cents carries the offset from the Asia desk, and the two must be read together rather than netted casually: "**the NAND side on the AI portion is extremely robust. There is no question about that. It's just in terms of bits, the non-AI part — the traditional tech consumer PC smartphone — that part is much larger than, let's say, on the DRAM side. And that's where we're seeing some weakness.**" The named soft markets are **smartphones in China, PC and consumer**, "weak all year and entering maybe recent one or two months finding a bit more challenging to push products **because it is becoming extremely expensive**" — i.e. price-driven demand destruction, not a demand cycle. Two supporting datapoints: **TrendForce inventory has ticked up since 2Q — the first time since 4Q last year — and it is at the MODULE MAKERS, not the producers** ("module makers did receive their commitments in terms of memory. But [are] finding a more challenging time to actually ship those in non-AI parts of memory, especially for NAND"); and **industry NAND bit growth is moving from high-teens to 20-25% next year**, with **YMTC adding a third line at 50,000 wafers**. For a NAND pure-play whose bit mix is not all enterprise, the risk is that the AI half of the market carries the ASP while the larger consumer half carries the bits — a mix that flatters price and pressures volume, and one that shows up in the channel before it shows up at the producer. Distinct from the standing "commodity cyclicality" bullet above: this is **channel inventory in the weak segment**, not a capacity-normalization argument. (Morgan Stanley webcast "2Q26 memory, Intel implications and ARM preview" · Sean, 2026-07-28) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_28_2q26_ms_memory_intel_implications_and_arm_preview.html)
- **Cannibalization tailwind maturing** — the eSSD-displaces-HDD vector that underwrites the AI-NAND TAM may be later-cycle than bulls assume: Bernstein puts the NAND/HDD ASP gap at a stabilized ~6-7x and peak datacenter NAND penetration likely behind us (Bernstein on HDD, 2025-10-23).
- **Competition** — Samsung, SK Hynix, YMTC and assemblers; YMTC (China) pressure on the low end (FY25 10-K). In eSSD specifically SNDK lags Micron and Hynix/Solidigm on share (MS AI NAND overview, 2025-09-10).
- **Kioxia JV dependence** — bit supply, cost and capex are tied to Flash Ventures; the $1.165B 2026–29 manufacturing payment + JV terms flow through COGS (Q2 FY26, 2026-01-29).
- **Customer concentration** — top-ten customers a large share of revenue (FY25 10-K); hyperscaler demand is lumpy.
- **Short standalone history** — limited as a public company since Feb-2025; thin comparables, evolving capital structure, residual WDC-separation items. Bernstein also flags SNDK's investor communications/disclosures as "confusing," a dissuader for quality-focused investors (Bernstein initiation, 2025-09-15).
- **China / geopolitics** — Unis Venture (China JV) restructuring; export/tariff exposure (FY25 10-K).
- **LTA execution risk** — backlog ($42B) and guarantees are only as good as counterparty follow-through if pricing rolls over.
- **HBF may disappoint** — early-stage, not HBM-drop-in, requires GPU redesign and ecosystem standardization; CSP adoption still tentative (MS AI NAND overview, 2025-09-10).
- **Insider selling into the rally** — Chief Accounting Officer Michael Pokorny sold 2,446 shares at $1,426.18/sh (~$3.5M, retains 22,375 shares ~$31M) and director Necip Sayiner (also on Rambus/Axcelis boards) sold 579 shares on 2026-05-08 at avg $1,503.11 (~$870K, retains 2,900 shares ~$4M) (Barron's "Inside Scoop," 2026-05-18). The dollar amounts are small relative to the run and read as gains-harvesting rather than a thesis-changing signal, but worth tracking as the stock keeps compounding.
- **Volatility / fragility of the re-rating** — the **-14% single-session drawdown on 2026-06-29** (vs SOX -7.9%), inside a week that closed -4.3%, is evidence the memory-cycle re-rating is high-beta and can air-pocket sharply on broad chip-sector risk-off, independent of company-specific news (Barron's "The Trader," 2026-06-29).

That is the single most important unquantified claim on this page — and it also cuts the BULL supply-sink trade (HBF tightening NAND and lifting industry pricing), which the same sentence denies.**
- **`NBM / LTA durability` — ✓ confirms, and extends a year further out than the page carried.** The row ends at 2/3 of FY28 bits. Visoso on FY29: *"**consistent with the ’28 number** so far… that’s why we didn’t put it there, because we’ll keep on **optimizing**"* — i.e. **~two-thirds coverage persists into FY29 and was withheld for optimisation reasons, not because it rolls off.** Also confirmed from the company rather than SIG: *"**quarter-by-quarter for the next 3 to 5 years**"* and *"in 2 quarters, we’ve gone from 3 months of visibility to 4 years."* And the capital-return ambiguity is closed — **"excess cash" = cash generated less investment in the business, returned 100%** ("there is no trick"), with Q1’s $5bn/$4.5bn as management’s worked example.
- 🔴 **NEW, AND IT DOES NOT FIT AN EXISTING ROW BECAUSE IT POINTS OFF THIS PAGE: management has taken a side in the memory-tiering debate, and the target is DRAM, not HBM.** On a 1.2tn-parameter internal run, *"having just the HBM SSD was good enough… you really required a **minute amount of DDR**"*; and on Vera Rubin *"cutting the system DRAM by half, the SOCAMM DRAM — **I would contend… you can go even further**."* ➔ **This page has been framing HBF-vs-HBM as the substitution question (MS against, Jefferies open). The company is arguing a DIFFERENT and larger substitution: eSSD + HBM displacing system DDR/SOCAMM. Cross-read [[MU]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[SKHYNIX]] on conventional DRAM demand and [[NVDA]] on platform BOM — do NOT fold it into the HBF-vs-HBM row.** ⚠️ **Vendor lab data, single workload, no methodology, obvious incentive — directional only, not a sizing input.**
- **Unchanged:** the multiple-expansion question, the "no through-cycle model" pushback, and the standing framing that HBF is optionality rather than a number. ⚠️ **UNIT GUARD added: SNDK’s own datacentre mix is ~1/3 (Moore, unchallenged by management) — that is NOT SIG’s "~50% of INDUSTRY bits in CY26." Company mix ≠ industry bit share.** 🆕 **ADDED 2026-08-21 — A BACKFILL, AND THE HANDLING IS THE POINT.** China Renaissance · Jack Zhou, "AI 101 Series: AI inference is reshaping the future of memory", 2026-04-30 (107pp, ~3.7 months stale) arrived late and was ingested **for its framework only**: **a new data-access layer between HBM/DRAM and traditional storage, flash going capacity-driven → performance-driven, TB/TCO → IOPS/TCO, and high-IOPS flash plus HBF at a 5-10x ASP uplift** — the cleanest third-party statement on this page of why HBF is a TIER rather than a faster SSD, and it independently reaches the same **[[KIOXIA]]-over-SanDisk** preference as Jefferies (04-07) and Redburn (06-23) from an architecture argument rather than a latency one. ⚠️⚠️ **ITS PRICE TARGET WAS *NOT* ALLOWED TO TOUCH THE THESIS. TP US$1,452 (raised from US$698) is logged as a HISTORICAL/SUPERSEDED mark with its 2026-04-30 date: it sits below every live target on this page and is 3.7 months older than all of them, so nothing was moved into the Changelog on its account. The Changelog is for values a NEW note supersedes — letting an older note push newer numbers into it would be backwards thesis drift. Same treatment for its April EPS series (FY26E/27E/28E US$52.48 / $181.50 / $191.31), recorded as vintage, not as estimates.** 🔴 **The one place its age is an ASSET: it timestamps the HBF slip from outside. In April it recorded SanDisk's own guidance as "HBF samples in 2HCY26, commercialization early-2027"; the Investor Day (08-13) landed on 2027 samples, no commercial date, and HBF outside the FY28-30 model. The goalposts moved ~two quarters right in four months — which is a cleaner statement of the HBF timing risk this page carries than any single current note provides.**

**🆕 Síntese — addendum 2026-08-25 (six sources; the demand leg gets confirmed by a competitor and the supply leg gets its first DATE).** Nothing above is superseded and no Sinal moves — but the shape of the debate on this page changes, because for the first time the bear case has a **funding source and a calendar**. ① **THE DEMAND MECHANISM IS CONFIRMED BY THE LARGEST COMPETITOR, UNPROMPTED, AND IT IS A PRODUCT-MIX CONCESSION: [[SAMSUNG]]'s DS spokesperson said Samsung is working “ONLY… on T[L]C SSD, NOT QLC” because “the KV cache needs mean that a lot of key GPU and hyperscalers are requiring high spec SSD products in order to OFFLOAD THE DATA WORKLOAD… FROM THE HBM to commodity,” naming NVIDIA and the ASIC/data-centre customers as placing “very high volume orders for TLC SSD”** (Samsung IR call via BTG, 2026-08-25). **Morgan Stanley independently models eSSD rising from 29% of NAND bits in 2025 to 65% in 2028 while CUTTING 2027 industry NAND bit-supply growth to 26% from 31%** (Morgan Stanley “Semiconductor Capital Equipment: 3Q'26 WFE update”, 2026-08-25). ② **AND THE SUPPLY-SINK BEAR NOW HAS AUDITED NUMBERS AND A DATE: YMTC filed for a ~$4.9bn Shanghai IPO disclosing 1Q revenue RMB47.04bn (~$7.0bn) — more than its entire 2024 — net profit RMB33.38bn in the quarter and a 77% gross margin vs 35% in 2025, with Counterpoint now ranking it No.3 globally in NAND SHIPMENTS and proceeds earmarked for line upgrades; TrendForce warns fresh capacity “could push the NAND market into OVERSUPPLY BY LATE 2027”** (WSJ, 2026-08-24). ⚠️ **That points OPPOSITE to MS's simultaneous bit-supply CUT; neither is netted, and the composition is the part that should worry the page — MS has YMTC at “approx. half of our greenfield adds in 2026/27.”** ③ **THE STREET MARK MOVED, BUT THE SHAPE SAYS THE FRANCHISE DID NOT: Mizuho cut SNDK only −1.3% to $1,875 AT AN UNCHANGED ~9x F27E EPS MULTIPLE — the smallest cut in a note that took [[MU]] down 5.5% and AMAT/MKSI down 9%/8% on DRAM/HBM de-spec — while reiterating GM >80% on NBM and EPS growing 5x F26-F28E. And the de-spec logic in that note is NAND-POSITIVE: lower memory content per accelerator “could allow ~20-30% MORE GPU/ASIC shipments” and higher KVCache NAND demand.** ④ **THE CLEANEST NEW READ ON THE PT LADDER IS A DECOMPOSITION: Mizuho's C27E EPS of $237.71 sits essentially ON Jefferies' C27 EPS of $238 — so the $1,875-vs-$3,000 gap between those two houses is ENTIRELY MULTIPLE, not estimates.** **Net: the eSSD demand leg is now corroborated by a competitor AND a third-party supply model; the open question has migrated from ‘is the demand real’ to ‘who supplies the 2027-28 bits’ — and the answer, on MS's own model, is roughly half China.**

Bernstein: *"Led by SANDISK and [[SKHYNIX]], HBF (high-bandwidth flash) aims to MATCH THE BANDWIDTH OF HBM but with lower cost"* and significantly higher capacity per bit, designed to COMPLEMENT rather than replace HBM, specifically for **AI INFERENCE** — raising overall bandwidth to the XPU.** 🔴 **THE MECHANICAL DETAIL THAT MATTERS COMMERCIALLY: *"Similar to DRAM dies stacked in HBM, NAND dies are stacked in HBF. HBF stacks then are MIXED WITH HBM STACKS and are also packaged with XPU with CoWoS-EQUIVALENT PACKAGING TECHNOLOGY"* — exhibit sourced to Sandisk itself.** ➤ **Two consequences worth carrying: (1) HBF sits INSIDE the accelerator package, so it competes for the same [[TSM]] CoWoS-class capacity as HBM — it is additive to the packaging constraint, not a way around it, which caps how fast it can ramp regardless of NAND supply; (2) it re-prices SNDK's NAND from a storage bit into an in-package AI component, which is the mechanism behind the gross-margin structure this page has been debating since the 08-13 investor day. [[SKHYNIX]]: equipment investment at DALIAN FAB 2 began in 3Q26, ~30,000 wpm, core tools including etchers arriving in volume FROM SEPTEMBER and running through 1H27 — reviving a project it broke ground on in 2022 and then shelved as NAND weakened.** ⚠️ **BOTH FIGURES CARRY THE OUTLET'S OWN GUARD AND IT IS THE CRITICAL QUALIFIER FOR THIS PAGE: for Samsung, “because the project involves CONVERTING AN EXISTING PRODUCTION LINE, that figure should NOT be read as an equivalent volume of entirely new wafer starts”; for SK hynix, “available information does NOT establish how much of the 30,000-wafer figure represents INCREMENTAL WAFER STARTS versus UPGRADED PRODUCTION.”** The outlet's own framing: both are **“extracting more value from manufacturing assets ALREADY IN CHINA through process migration and selective equipment upgrades rather than committing to entirely new NAND fabs”** — higher layer counts raise bits per wafer without matching wafer-start growth. **UBS adds the process detail on the SK hynix side: Dalian additions would be on FLOATING-GATE technology, potentially moving to 240L from 144/192L, and M17 (NAND) is “expected to start MP in 1H29.”** ➤ **1H29 for the next major Korean NAND greenfield is a long way out and is supportive of the allocation environment this page depends on.** 🔴 **THE RISK LEG, WHICH IS NEW TO THIS PAGE: Reuters (Aug 5) — US Commerce designated the Samsung and SK Hynix China fabs Validated End Users in 2023 and REVOKED that status in 2025; both then received an ANNUAL authorisation covering 2026 only, leaving “LESS VISIBILITY beyond the current authorization period,” with the specific concern that restrictions could extend to SERVICING, REPAIR AND REPLACEMENT of already-installed Western tools. ➤ A tool-access shock at Xi'an or Dalian would be a NAND SUPPLY SHOCK and therefore a positive for SNDK pricing — an asymmetric option the page did not previously hold.** ⚠️ **Both companies DENY the associated report that they evaluated AMEC etch tools as a hedge.** [Sources](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Samsung_SK_Hynix_step_up_China_NAND_upgrades_as_US_tool_risks_grow.html) · [UBS](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/UBS_000660KS_First_Read_SK_Hynix_Key_Takeaways_from_UBS_Korea_Summi.html) |

No SNDK rating or PT in it** | produto | **mixed** | 🔴 **THE FIRST HARD $/GB FRAMING OF HBF ON THIS PAGE, AND IT COMES FROM THE SCEPTIC'S SIDE OF THE TABLE, WHICH IS WHY IT IS WORTH MORE THAN A PROPONENT'S: “GOOGL/Tenstorrent partnering with SNDK/Hynix on HBF showing 3Tbps bandwidth (vs HBM ~1.6-2Tbps), potentially HBF ~$1/GB, vs HBM4e at ~$30/GB.”** ➤ **~30x cheaper per GB at ~1.5-2x the bandwidth is the clearest statement of the HBF value proposition this page has — and the named partner set (GOOGL and Tenstorrent alongside SNDK and SK hynix) is new here. ⚠️ It is MIZUHO'S CHARACTERISATION of a Micron meeting, NOT a SanDisk disclosure and NOT a Google/Tenstorrent announcement.** ⚠️ **AND THE COMPETITOR'S REBUTTAL, WHICH IS THE HONEST OTHER HALF: Micron sees “HBF SUBSTITUTING HBM UNLIKELY as HBF still has some challenges” — “endurance/latency, R/W errors/write speed, versus HBM unparalleled R/W speeds/NO errors” — “BUT WILL SEE ADOPTION IN THE MEMORY STACK,” with “AI tiered memory… [seeing] HBF demand with context lengths/KVcache demand.” Micron also puts CXL memory pooling “2-3 YEARS AWAY.”** ➤ **This is the same verdict the independent technical teardown reaches from the physics (see the `## Debate / thesis` archive bullet below): HBF is a TIER between HBM and SSD, not an HBM replacement. Two independent sources — one commercial, one technical — landing in the same place is the reason this page should keep treating HBF as OPTIONALITY WITH A SHAPE rather than a binary.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Takeaways_-_Supply_Tight_into_2028E_80_GMs_Sustainable_T.html) |

Plus the tokenomics exhibit: **OpenAI, Anthropic and Google all price cached tokens at exactly 10% of compute tokens** (DeepSeek ~1-2%; legacy gpt-4o 50%) — the price signal underwriting KV-cache offload. Ref px $1,350.50, so $1,750 is still +30% to PT.** **The estimate cut is the substance and it is a 2027 cut, not a 2026 cut: Cal-2026 revenue +2% / EPS +4% vs prior JEFe, but Cal-2027 revenue −14% / EPS −14%. Versus consensus, Jefferies now sits 2026 −1% rev / +3% EPS and 2027 −12% rev / −10% EPS — i.e. it has moved from above the Street to BELOW it on 2027.** Quarterly EPS path revised: **Q3 $46.07 (prev $44.60), Q4 $51.57 (prev $53.32), Cal-26 $160.29 (prev $154.60)** — so the near quarters went UP and the target came down 42%: **the cut is entirely multiple/2027-durability, not near-term earnings.** ⚠️ **The mechanism Curtis flags is the one to carry: "Edge remained the largest $ contributor to Jun/q growth, up nearly 400% y/y and reaching 61% OF REVENUE. As outlined in our preview, we think it's likely these are AGGRESSIVE INVENTORY BUILDS THAT WILL EVENTUALLY TRANSITION INTO HEADWINDS over the coming quarters."** That is a direct challenge to the quality of the beat — 61% of revenue in the segment he believes is channel-stuffed. **"We maintain that Data Center will be the primary growth engine going forward, but it's reasonable to expect a MORE MODEST BIT GROWTH OUTLOOK as the story plays out."** On margins: **"GMs also guided lower as the impact of LOWER-GM LTAs offset favorable pricing dynamics and raises the question of WHAT GM SNDK USES IN ITS UPDATED OPERATING MODEL"** — i.e. the NBM structure is now visibly diluting the margin model, which is the cost side of the durability trade. NBM positives retained: 8 customers, >50% of F27 and ~67% of F28 bits committed; NAND TAM >$300B C26 → ~$500B C27. **Analyst Day Thursday 2026-08-13, NYC.** (Jefferies · Blayne Curtis, 2026-08-06) |
| **08-06** | **⚠️ Susquehanna · Mehdi Hosseini — "Normalization Requires Give-and-Take While the Mix Requires Better Execution" · POSITIVE, PT $3,050 → $2,745 (−10%)** | **margem** | **mixed** | **Second PT cut of the day; rating held at Positive, downside risk $1,000 flagged. Ref px $1,350.50, mkt cap $200.0bn.** **The frame is the most useful bear-lite argument on the page because it is about ELASTICITY, not a cycle top: "As AI spending increasingly transitions from TRAINING TO INFERENCE, ASPs and OM are likely to NORMALIZE from the recent highs, requiring a greater balance between pricing, profitability, and DEMAND STIMULATION across the value chain. Investors may need to recalibrate expectations as the industry moves beyond the unusually favorable pricing and margin dynamics of the recent AI-TRAINING-LED cycle."** **The concrete mechanism, and it is a NEW one for this page: "As SanDisk begins shipping 2Tb QLC-based high-capacity SSDs, with demand increasingly driven by KV CACHE OFFLOADING, both bit shipments and product mix are likely to create N-T volatility that is difficult to model with confidence. To accelerate adoption of these high-capacity SSDs, SanDisk will likely need to offer customers MORE COMPELLING ECONOMICS. This does not imply broad-based pricing concessions, but rather TARGETED PRICING ADJUSTMENTS OF A FEW CENTS (PER GB) to improve value propositions and drive volume growth acceleration."** So: cheaper $/GB deliberately, to buy elasticity in the KV-cache offload use case — which is exactly the demand driver [[MU]] described at FMS the same week. **Hosseini is explicit that this is NOT a call for an ASP correction: "These dynamics do not suggest that the NAND industry is on the verge of a significant ASP correction. Rather, we view this as part of a broader 'NORMALIZATION' process in which demand elasticity, particularly for KV cache offloading applications, drives an ACCELERATION IN NAND BIT GROWTH."** ⚠️ **The consumer flag is the softest datapoint in the flow: "the consumer electronics market appears to be approaching a POINT OF DEMAND RESISTANCE, as consumers become less willing to absorb higher prices associated with rising BOM costs"** — pairs with the Windows-11/32GB-RAM recommendation withdrawal (TechRadar, 08-06) and Apple's own reluctant Mac/iPad price increases (see [[AAPL]]). **On the GM miss: "SanDisk guided to HIGHER DOI for the Sep-Q to support long-term agreements"** — the same inventory-build-for-NBM mechanism, from a second independent source. (Susquehanna · Mehdi Hosseini, 2026-08-06) |

THE 08-13 ANALYST DAY IS WHERE THIS GETS SETTLED — the HBF roadmap is the billed content. Competitive note: [[SAMSUNG]] unveiled zNAND-O at FMS, bringing HBF to EDGE AI, with Qualcomm and Google "already evaluating adoption," though TrendForce says consumer commercialisation "will ultimately hinge on COST COMPETITIVENESS" (@trendforce, 2026-08-06) — the same cost objection, from the other end of the market.** |

Newman / April Li / Phoebe Sun — "Memory & Storage: What we learned from our NDR with STX" — SNDK read-through: OUTPERFORM, PT $3,000 RE-MARKED (the page's staleness flag on this mark is resolved), and the LTA argument runs EXACTLY OPPOSITE to Jefferies' on the same day** | valuation / margem | **bull** | ✅ **THE STALENESS FLAG IS CLEARED: this page listed "Bernstein $3,000" as *stale — not re-marked this window* on 08-06. Bernstein re-marks it on 08-07 at OUTPERFORM, PT $3,000, valuing SNDK "at $3000 on 11x FY28 EPS, or 14x through-cycle EPS (FY26-30 avg EPS)." Bernstein's numbers: TTM adj EPS $70.08; FY27E $238.36; FY28E $272.09; implied P/E 18.0x / 5.3x / 4.6x on the 6-Aug close of $1,258.58. This restores Bernstein as the HIGHEST LIVE MARK on the page (vs Susquehanna $2,745, BofA $2,500, GS $2,200, MS/Jefferies $1,750).** 🔴 **THE ANALYTICALLY IMPORTANT PART IS THAT IT IS THE MIRROR IMAGE OF THE JEFFERIES CALL LOGGED DIRECTLY BELOW, ON THE SAME DAY, FROM THE SAME FACT: "we maintain Outperform rating on SNDK and still see SIGNIFICANT UPSIDE PARTICULARLY AS 2/3 OF VOLUMES ON LTAs WITH 80% FLOOR GROSS MARGIN SHOULD HELP IMPROVE EARNINGS STABILITY." And, on the FQ4 print: "While some may worry its growing LTA coverage CAPS SOME NEAR-TERM UPSIDE AND IS SLOWING ASP GROWTH, we believe it is important to note that GREATER LTA COVERAGE IS POSITIVE FOR THE STOCK'S MULTIPLE AND RE-RATING POTENTIAL: LTAs provide ROBUST DOWNSIDE PROTECTION, make NOSEBLEED EARNINGS MORE SUSTAINABLE, and should REDUCE PERCEIVED CYCLICALITY."** ⚠️ **Set this against Blayne Curtis the same day — "if you're signing deals at 80% GROSS MARGIN, YOUR ASPs ARE NOT GOING UP AS MUCH… when you figure that out, THE MULTIPLE CONTRACTS." Two houses, one fact (2/3 of volume at an 80% floor GM), opposite conclusions on the MULTIPLE: Bernstein says lower cyclicality → higher multiple; Jefferies says capped upside → lower multiple. That is the cleanest statement of the live SNDK debate on this page, and it is a multiple argument, not an earnings argument.** **Bernstein's stated downside risks on SNDK are unchanged and worth keeping next to the bull case: near-term numbers look high and face cyclical NAND downside; disclosures/investor communications "have been confusing, potentially dissuading more quality-focused investors"; and NAND weakness could prove structural rather than cyclical, in which case the DCF value is structurally lower and impaired asset value "may be materially below replacement cost."** **Context from the HDD side of the same note, relevant to the NAND-vs-HDD tier debate this page carries: Seagate management frames NAND/SSD as essential for high-performance workloads, KV cache and WARM storage, but "warm storage represents only a small portion of total exabytes, whereas COLD STORAGE REMAINS SIGNIFICANTLY LARGER."** (Bernstein · Mark C. WE DIDN'T DOWNGRADE IT, BUT WE DID CUT OUR PRICE TARGET PRETTY HEAVILY."** ⚠️ **Context from the host, unchallenged: SNDK "was still your top pick for the second half of 2026… when we kind of flipped the calendar several weeks ago." A top pick keeping its rating while losing its target is the shape of a thesis being narrowed rather than abandoned — and note the desk's own 08-05 first-look row below still carries BUY / TP $3,000, so THE PT ON THIS PAGE IS NOW STALE PENDING THE PUBLISHED NUMBER. No new target is stated on the call; do not infer one.** 🔴 **WHY — THE EARNINGS CEILING IS THE POINT, AND IT IS AN LTA ARGUMENT: "you're doing these LONG-TERM AGREEMENTS. BUT YOU'RE DOING IT AT LOWER MARGIN THAN YOU'RE CURRENTLY DOING IN THE OPEN MARKET… if you're modeling, you're like, OKAY, NOW I KNOW THAT PART OF THE EQUATION IS NOT GOING TO UPSIDE ME. And oh, by the way, there's math here — IF YOU'RE SIGNING DEALS AT 80% GROSS MARGIN, YOUR ASPs ARE NOT GOING UP AS MUCH. So then you look at where the street's modeled and where expectations were — PEOPLE WERE TALKING ABOUT $3 TO $500 OF EARNINGS. YOU KNOW THE UNIT GROWTH IS HIGH TEENS IN THE MARKET, THEY'RE GOING TO UNDERGROW IT. And now we know that THE ASPs ARE A LITTLE SOFTER, you can start to ENCAPSULATE WHAT THE UPSIDE IS. And when you figure that out, THE MULTIPLE CONTRACTS."** 🔴 **THE CONCLUSION IS PERMANENT, NOT CYCLICAL: "I JUST DON'T THINK YOU'RE GOING TO GET THAT CREDIT FOR $3 TO $500 OF EARNINGS EVER, because I just think that you're STARTING TO SEE THAT MATURITY CURVE ON MARGINS AND PRICING."** ⚠️ **"Ever" is the word that matters. This is the first argument on this page that the $300-500 EPS bull case is not merely delayed but structurally unreachable, and the mechanism is the LTAs themselves — the thing the bull case treats as de-risking. Cross-filed [hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md) where the LTA-as-cycle-extender argument sits.** ⚠️ **ON THE LTA FRAMEWORK GENERALLY: "there's still that camp that… HAVE ENDORSED THESE LTAs AS A GAME CHANGER.
