| 08-02 | **Jefferies · Blayne Curtis — Q2 preview into the 08-05 PM print, "Beat & Raise but Supply/Demand Commentary the Focus"** | guidance | **mixed** | **★ THE BUY-SIDE BAR, STATED AS A NUMBER, PLUS THE FIRST CREDIBLE ACCOUNT OF WHY THE ASP EXTRAPOLATION IS BREAKING DOWN. "Initial buyside expectations for Sep/Q guide range from $50-60 OF EPS based on ASP GROWTH OF 25-30%."** ⚠️ **The sentence that matters: "In prior quarters, investors had expressed a willingness to EXTRAPOLATE THAT SAME ASP GROWTH THROUGH C27 but WE ARE SEEING SOME SIGNS THIS STANCE MAY BE SOFTENING." That is the mechanism behind a −15% week on a name in an up-cycle — the multiple is being taken off the out-year ASP curve, not off the quarter.** **THE DEMAND-DESTRUCTION DECOMPOSITION IS NET-NEW AND IT IS THE SHARPEST BEAR CASE ON THIS PAGE: "concerns around potential impact from DEMAND DESTRUCTION in consumer electronics, PCs, Smartphones, and Enterprise servers are pushing to the forefront." Two exposures sized: (1) "SNDK has ~50%+ MARKET SHARE OF THE CONSUMER MARKET representing 14% OF TOTAL SALES in Mar/Q where REVENUE FELL 10% Q/Q" — the highest-share, most price-elastic end market is already declining; (2) ⚠️ "EDGE EXPOSURE IS PERHAPS THE GREATER CONCERN representing 62% OF REVENUE IN MARCH while growing NEARLY 120% Q/Q as customers RACED TO BUILD INVENTORY AHEAD OF ANTICIPATED PRICE INCREASES. At a certain point, the Edge has built enough inventory and THAT REVENUE WILL BEGIN TO FALL OFF, and though the Data Center business for SNDK should continue to grow it's possible we [see] some HEADWINDS TO BIT GROWTH before DC is large enough to offset."** **Read that plainly: ~62% of the revenue base grew 120% q/q on a pull-forward, and the analyst previewing a beat-and-raise is simultaneously describing the air pocket on the other side of it. The beat is a timing artefact by his own account.** ⚠️ **THIS COMPOUNDS the 08-01 Susquehanna row below rather than offsetting it: Hosseini's finding was that SNDK's own fab partner has publicly decided high-capacity QLC "remains TOO COSTLY for broad deployment" while [[SAMSUNG]] more than doubles QLC bits in 2H26 — so the supply-side competitive risk and the demand-side pull-forward risk are now both live into the same print.** One directional positive Jefferies retains, and it is a REVERSAL of his own prior stance: **"in terms of supply, he's have been BULLISH NAND VS. DRAM given the concentration from Memory vendors on prioritizing DRAM expansion vs. NAND BUT THIS TREND MAY BE SHIFTING"** — i.e. the NAND supply-discipline argument that underwrote the ASP curve is being questioned by the analyst who made it. **He "expect[s] any potential impact would be more TEMPORAL in nature and remain[s] bullish" longer-term.** Corroborating price data the same weekend: **TrendForce's updated July contract prices have DRAM UP LOW-TEENS% MoM (30-50% q/q) but NAND FLAT** (BofA · Simon Woo, 2026-08-02) — NAND is not participating in the July contract move, which is consistent with the ASP-extrapolation softening. See [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md), [[KIOXIA]], [[SKHYNIX]]. |
| 08-01 | **Susquehanna · Mehdi Hosseini (Tech Hardware weekly) — the QLC roadmap into the 08-05 print** | supply | **bull** | **★ The competitive-supply picture SanDisk reports into on 08-05 AMC, assembled from the three suppliers that printed ahead of it. Hosseini's call: "the QLC supply ramp appears to be SHIFTING SLIGHTLY TO THE RIGHT, though it REMAINS ON TRACK TO ACCELERATE IN 2H26 as suppliers migrate to ultra-high-density nodes."** ⚠️ **The right-shift is the near-term positive for pricing and the 2H26 acceleration is the medium-term risk — and the three competitors are pursuing visibly DIFFERENT strategies, which is the part to carry: [[SAMSUNG]] is the aggressor (QLC bit shipments in 2H26 to MORE THAN DOUBLE vs 1H26, on the new V9 2Tb QLC plus V10 V-NAND MASS PRODUCTION SCHEDULED FOR AUGUST 2026, explicitly "to address undersupplied AI infrastructure"); [[KIOXIA]] — SanDisk's own JV partner — is deliberately NOT chasing it ("continues to prioritize TLC eSSDs for performance-critical inferencing, leveraging its 8th-gen BiCS FLASH, as high-capacity QLC solutions remain TOO COSTLY for broad deployment"); [[SKHYNIX]] is on node migration (321-layer to 50% of domestic production by year-end to support surging eSSD demand).** ⚠️ **The Kioxia divergence is the one that matters most for this page, because the two companies share fabs: if Kioxia's read that QLC is too costly for broad deployment is right, the JV's bit mix stays TLC-weighted and SanDisk's QLC positioning is constrained by its partner's capital allocation, not only by its own. This is a question for the 08-05 call.** **Demand-side timing from the customer layer: SIMO expects QLC to begin ramping toward end-2026 through FIVE TIER-1 CSP ENGAGEMENTS for its MonTitan platform, with adoption "becoming more meaningful in 2027-28" as 2Tb dies become widely available and SSDs reach the DENSITY CROSSOVER POINT needed to displace HDDs.** ⚠️ **Note the crossover framing cuts against the [[STX]]/[[WDC]] bull case logged the same day ("pace of SSD replacing nearline HDD is slowing"; rising NAND prices widen the HDD cost advantage) — the two industries are telling opposite stories about the same substitution, with SanDisk's side dated 2027-28 and the HDD side dated "next five years." Logged unresolved.** **More colour expected at FMS this week; Hosseini carries SNDK Positive at $1,214.83.** (Susquehanna · Mehdi Hosseini, 2026-08-01) |

| 07-31 | **BofA · Simon Woo / Dai Shen / Vivek Arya — Global Memory Tech weekly** | supply | **bull** | **★ The LTA mechanics, and they invert the common bear reading of high contract coverage. "LTA does not fix memory chip price… lot of room to raise contract price every quarter but there is LIMIT FOR PRICE CUT." Specifically: Samsung said 60-70% OF ITS MEMORY SALES WILL BE UNDER LTA, but with ASYMMETRIC terms — a price-cut FLOOR of sub-5% down q/q against price INCREASES broadly open at 10-20%+. And on tenor: Samsung's LTAs (mainly for US Big Tech) are mostly 5-YEAR ROLLING, so a 6th year can be added right after or before the 1st year completes.** ⚠️ **So LTAs here are not price locks — they are DOWNSIDE COLLARS WITH UNCAPPED UPSIDE on a rolling tenor. High LTA coverage is therefore bullish for realised ASP stability rather than a cap on it, which is the opposite of how rising contract coverage is usually read at this point in a cycle.** **Spot: sentiment positive into August on three drivers — (1) chip order increases for inventory restocking; (2) many Tier-2 OEMs resuming smartphone/PC launches for Sept/4Q DESPITE high memory cost; (3) China domestic smartphone shipments bottoming out — only 17MN UNITS IN JUNE vs a normal 25-30mn, with lean channel inventories. ⚠️ Note the DRAM/NAND divergence in the confirmed July contract prints: TrendForce July DRAM contract price UP LOW-TEENS % M/M = 30-50% Q/Q, but FLAT FOR NAND.** **On China supply, the reason it is not showing up in spot: "minimal impact from China local chipmakers' capacity expansion — China-branded memory NOT COMMONLY TRADED IN THE SPOT MARKET; even Korea/US/Japan chips not much available for white-box OEMs."** Adjacent confirmation that the AI-server pull is broad: **SEMCO and LG Innotek both guided bullishly on AI MLCC and FC-BGA, with 2Q OP more than doubling y/y, stronger LTA orders, LARGE PREPAYMENTS funding new fab construction, and contract price increases through 2H and 2027 — and all three top MLCC makers (SEMCO, Murata, Taiyo Yuden) have raised prices.** |

Sep-Q guided to revenue ¥2.39tn / OP ¥1.9tn vs ¥1.95tn consensus.** Jefferies argues the miss is optical against a bar already marked down — the stock went **limit up pre-print**, sold off after hours, then recovered to ~+3%. **The bullish part, and it is the part that matters for SNDK: ASP +70% Q/Q LIKE-FOR-LIKE on only LOW-SINGLE-DIGIT BIT GROWTH — the entire revenue move is price, and the volume restraint is deliberate. The cycle is being carried entirely by datacenter; consumer is not contributing.** Corroborating the datacenter pull from a different angle: **[[LRCX]] says data centres increasingly demand SLC/TLC (not QLC) NAND for performance in AI memory stacks — more expensive, requiring more capacity, "a must" — but teed up for '28-'30, with NAND WFE actually DOWN 1H'26 vs 1H'25 in dollars.** |

Plus the first channel-inventory signal in three quarters: **TrendForce inventory up since 2Q, first time since 4Q last year, and "it's mostly the module makers. Supply is also loosening at the margin: **industry NAND bit growth high-teens → 20-25% next year, with YMTC adding a third line and 50,000 wafers.** Net read for a NAND pure-play: **ASP from the AI half, bit risk from the consumer half** (see Risks). **Quarter synthesis:** the price/margin cycle and the NBM structure are broadly confirmed by the flow (SNDK as price-setter, undersupply through end-CY27); the debate has shifted to the tail — valuation on 2028 numbers (MS prefers Micron, some 2028 LTAs priced below 2027) and the competitive latency risk in enterprise (Redburn: Kioxia Super IO vs SNDK's HPF only in 2028). Jefferies' PT raise to $3,000 (06-27) and eSSD share recapture (4%→8%) were the first actionable bull datapoints; Stargate QLC launch is the near-term catalyst watch. The KIS/@jukan05 note (06-29) adds a structural point to the durability side: it was customers, not suppliers, who first proposed the 2028+ LTAs — read as evidence the shortage runs long — and confirms the LTA structure now varies by supplier (SK Hynix floor/no-ceiling vs Samsung/Micron), the same floor/ceiling architecture SNDK disclosed on its NBMs. Layered on top, the mainstream-press/tape read (Barron's, 05-18 → 06-01 → 06-29) tracks the stock's own re-rating arc — +465% to +523% YTD, an explicit 1999-2000 tech-boom analog, modest insider selling, and then a -14% single-session drawdown (vs SOX -7.9%) — a reminder that even with the LTA durability argument intact, the equity itself remains high-beta and prone to sharp air-pockets on broad chip-sector risk-off. **Turn of the month (06-29 → 07-01) brought a second wave of estimate raises from a different set of banks**: Bernstein's $3,000 PT (06-29, restated with consensus math 06-30: base EPS +27-28% above Street, bull +84-87%) and BofA's new $2,500 PO (Wamsi Mohan, 07-01, FY27E EPS +24% to $233.07 / FY28E EPS +14.5% to $254.24) are **three distinct banks' targets** (Jefferies $3,000 06-27, Bernstein $3,000 06-29, BofA $2,500 07-01) that should not be conflated despite the round-number overlap between Jefferies and Bernstein. BofA's desk (Fenske) called the 40-50% FY28 EPS revision across SNDK/STX/WDC an unusually large one-day raise — yet Jefferies (Favuzza) flagged SNDK trading -3.8% pre-market the same morning (07-01) as part of a broader leader/laggard rotation, even after being the single best-performing >$5bn stock in 1H26 (+765%) and a top-6 YTD contributor to both the Nasdaq 100 and S&P 500. Net: fundamental estimate revisions and price action are diverging session-to-session — the durability thesis is being re-underwritten by the sell-side even as the tape itself rotates away from the name. **Into the 2Q print (07-03/07-05) the re-underwriting continued:** UBS (Gaudois, Memory Semis Monthly, 07-03) raised its NAND contract-pricing path to +30%/+12% QoQ 3Q/4Q26 (3Q up from +17%) after +67% in 2Q and pushed the NAND upcycle out to at least 4Q27 — a direct supply-side corroboration of SNDK's undersupply thesis (though UBS's NAND Buys are the Asian names, not SNDK). GS (Schneider, 07-05) then raised its SNDK PT to $2,200 (from $1,200) at 20x normalized EPS $110 (from $55), with CY26 EPS >~30% above Street — making GS the fourth distinct bank in a two-week window to lift numbers, and reinforcing that the debate is duration (NAND tight potentially longer than DRAM), not direction. **Bernstein's part-2 note (Newman, 07-09)** then reframes the durability case historically: by benchmarking the New Memory LTAs against two *failed* legacy structures (Microchip's no-cash PSP that collapsed into an analog glut; Hemlock's legally-airtight-but-uncollectible polysilicon take-or-pay), it argues SNDK's contracts differ on three structural axes — upfront back-end-weighted collateral (coverage → 75-100% of RPO in the outer years), investment-grade counterparties, and structural AI demand — restating SNDK's ~$69B RPO / >$11B guarantees and reiterating PT $3,000 with the "meaningful but not infinite" downside-protection framing. It is the clearest articulation yet of *why* the LTA layer should carry a higher multiple, but explicitly stops short of claiming a floor at any price. **07-20 (two same-day PTs, opposite ends of the range):** Bernstein (Rasgon, "WFE / AI GW note," Outperform **$3,000**, px $1,354.82, EPS 26E/27E $65.43/$243.73) and MS (Joe Moore, "US memory selloff = entry point," OW **$1,750**, 28x through-cycle EPS $62.50, FY Jun27e GM 86.7% / EPS $214.73) both reiterated the same day — a **~$1,250 dispersion between two brokers' Street targets** that captures the unresolved 2028-durability debate on this page (both reaffirm figures already carried; neither supersedes). Bernstein frames SNDK a direct AI-data beneficiary whose LTAs dampen cyclicality → re-rate; MS sees NAND improving quickly on cloud + eSSD. **07-28 (MS memory webcast) — the NAND market has bifurcated, and the split is the most useful thing added to this page since the print.** On the strong side, **Joe Moore reports enterprise NAND transacting above 70 cents on multiple deals, up 60-70% from Q2, after describing the market as "wobbly" only a month earlier — and explicitly notes that the LTA pushback and tough Q3 negotiations of a month ago "completely changed."** That reversal lands on this page's central durability debate (MS's own 06-22 flag that some 2028 enterprise-TLC LTA prices were *down* vs 2027 was the sharpest form of that worry), and the transacted level runs ahead of both BofA's +35% q/q F4Q26 ASP preview and UBS's +30% q/q 3Q26 contract call. On the weak side, **Sean scopes it precisely: AI NAND is "extremely robust," but "in terms of bits, the non-AI part — the traditional tech consumer PC smartphone — that part is much larger" and it is soft, on price-driven demand destruction rather than a demand cycle** (China smartphones, PC, consumer, "weak all year"). Supporting that: **TrendForce inventory ticked up since 2Q — the first time since 4Q last year — at module makers, not producers**, and industry **NAND bit growth moves high-teens → 20-25% next year** on YMTC's third line (50,000 wafers). For a NAND pure-play the composition matters more than the direction: **ASP is coming from the AI half, bit risk is accumulating in the larger consumer half, and the channel is where it shows first.** Finally, positioning: **Joe ranks SNDK fourth in his own coverage — NVDA > AVGO > MU > SNDK** — so the strongest NAND pricing datapoint of the quarter arrives from an analyst who ranks the pure-play last of four. Both facts are true and neither cancels the other; no rating, PT or estimate superseded.

| **08-25** | **[[KIOXIA]] read-across (Goldman Sachs IR conf. call) — not a Sandisk note** | **supply / guidance** | **bull** | **Kioxia’s LTA disclosure is the cleanest available comparator for Sandisk’s NBMs, and the structures differ in a way that matters.** Kioxia: **coverage ~50% of CY28 bit shipments (>50% for CY27)**, and agreements **"commonly secure a certain return on investment WITHOUT SETTING A CEILING, enabling the company to capture some upside when market prices rise."** ➤ **Sandisk’s NBMs carry BOTH a floor and a ceiling (per the Investor Day and Bristlemoon’s 08-28 work filed above); Kioxia’s, as described, are floor-only. If both descriptions are accurate, Kioxia retains more upside in a sustained tight market while Sandisk has traded that upside for a harder floor — a genuine structural difference in how the two have chosen to de-cyclicalise, and one to test on the next Sandisk disclosure.** Kioxia also **reaffirmed supply/demand tightness through CY27** with **no assumption of like-for-like price declines**, ASP increases moderating but positive, and bit shipments rising q/q even in the seasonally weak 4Q of FY3/27 — **supportive read-across for the near end of Sandisk’s curve, which is the part Bristlemoon says is NOT the problem.** (Goldman Sachs "Kioxia Holdings (285A.T): Conf. call: Reaffirms view that supply/demand tightness will last through CY27; LTAs emphasize margins; reiterate Buy", Shuhei Nakamura / Kaho Otake, 2026-08-25). | **08-28** | 🔴🔴 **Bristlemoon Capital — "Making sense of Sandisk’s long-term financial model" (independent buy-side; no rating)** | **guidance / margem / valuation** | **mixed** | ★★ **The most rigorous outside attack yet on the FY28-FY30 long-term model, and it lands exactly on the divergence this wiki has been carrying. Its conclusion is not "the model is too high" — it is that THE MODEL IS INTERNALLY INCONSISTENT AND SHOULD BE DE-EMPHASISED.** **The NBM facts first: 8 strategic customers, minimum contracted revenue $94bn over 4 years at floor prices, fixed + variable (floor AND ceiling) pricing, volume commitments growing over the 4-year weighted-average duration; ~half of FY27 bits and ~two-thirds of FY28 bits covered.** **Bristlemoon’s derived floor: ~$14bn minimum contracted FY27 revenue on the 50% of bits under NBM → ~$28bn total, against a 4Q26 annualised run-rate of $36bn growing to $38-39bn on +7-8% bits at flat ASP. That implies the NBM floor price sits 26-28% BELOW realised 4Q26 ASP, and a gross-margin floor of 78-79% against 4Q26’s 84.6%** — corroborated by CFO **Luis Visoso**, who confirmed at the Investor Day that NBM floor gross margin is **"around 80%."** 🔴 **THE INCONSISTENCY: management guides mid-high-teens annual BIT growth with discipline, which makes the mid-high-teens revenue growth in the FY28-FY30 model bit-driven with FLAT average ASP. But "the ~80% average gross margin is only plausible if ASP falls by over 30% from today’s levels, after accounting for annual bit cost downs." The only reconciliation is that gross margin ENTERS FY28 at 80% — i.e. blended ASP has already fallen to the NBM floor or below — which makes the "sustainable model" a bet that the NBMs hold in an oversupplied market.** The alternative (84% in FY28, averaging 80%) requires later margins **below the NBM floor**, which would be *"either a direct repudiation of the effectiveness of the NBMs, or non-NBM ASP and margins crash so hard that one must question why hyperscale customers are eagerly signing 4-5 year LTAs in the first place."* ➤ **Verdict: "the long-term model as presented is a piece of investor marketing material that should be de-emphasized because actual performance will either be better or MATERIALLY WORSE."** **The scenarios, with numbers:** (1) supply accelerates past the mid-high-teens target, NBM ASP to the floor and non-NBM ASP **-50% by 4Q29 vs 4Q26** → **FY29 gross margin 78%, EPS $218**; (2) **NBMs breached**, both NBM and non-NBM ASP -50% → **gross margin 72%, EPS $154.** At **8x** (one turn above the current ~7x forward P/E) that is a **"trough" share price of $1,200-$1,700 in mid-2028 vs $1,485 today** — i.e. even the breach case is not obviously a disaster. A sub-$100 FY29 EPS needs **ASP -75% peak-to-trough.** ⚠️ **The sensitivity warning is the durable takeaway: "a ~MSD% bit deficit has been sufficient to push NAND ASPs up FOUR-FOLD over the past year. Conversely, a MSD% bit surplus could in theory drive a similar collapse" — error bars alone swing ASP by multiples, so point forecasts of FY29-30 are not meaningful.** **Where Bristlemoon lands NET LONG the cycle:** KV-cache offload and long-horizon agentic memory are *"material and entirely incremental"* NAND demand — **Sandisk estimates a 1 ZB installed base of KV-cache-offload storage by 2030, absorbing a high-teens % of industry bit supply over 4 years** (vs ~1.2 ZB current industry capacity); Bristlemoon judges that aggressive but still models **incremental mid-high-teens annual bit demand** from KV-cache + agentic memory, before any AI-video storage. **Supply check:** [[SKHYNIX]] 50% Dalian expansion, [[SAMSUNG]] Pyeongtaek P5, [[KIOXIA]] Kitakami K3 all online **2027-2029+**; the wildcard is **YMTC at ~200kwpm (~15% industry share) planning three new 100kwpm mostly-NAND fabs, first 50kwpm operational 2027** — **300kwpm would be a 20% lift to industry wafer capacity, but spread over 5 years only ~4%/yr.** Conclusion: *"unlikely that existing high-teens bit production growth plus these new capacity additions will be sufficient to tip the market into oversupply within the next 2-3 years."* **Tape context Bristlemoon cites: the stock "initially pumped over 40% post-Investor Day and has since given up more than half of those gains."** (Bristlemoon Capital "Making sense of Sandisk’s long-term financial model", 2026-08-28). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/sndk_bristlemoon.html) |

**🆕 08-09/10 — HBF gets its first numbers *and* its first serious engineering audit, in the same week.** `produto · mixed` — SNDK's own modelling (4 HBF GPUs ≈ 8 HBM GPUs on throughput; ~8x min-config capital efficiency) is the most concrete pro-HBF datapoint the name has produced, and UBS carried it without dissent. But the 130-page base-die spec that landed alongside it reads, to a hardware-literate reader, like a **PHY-limited design** (8G UCIe as an official option when everyone has 64G PHYs running; reliability specs deferred), and the most bullish engineering verdict available is "might work but is not optimal" (Irrational Analysis, 08-09). **The bull case that did not need HBF at all is the one that got stronger:** persistent KV cache as an *SSD* demand driver (~1ZB by C2030 per SNDK; >70% of C27E industry bits per UBS) plus the 2Tb QLC mass-capacity inflection SIG is underwriting. Watch that the equity does not get re-rated on the HBF option while the earnings actually come from QLC eSSD.

Together with a flat-to-down GM guide, management effectively signalled the ASP-escalation leg is flattening, and Jefferies reports it "implicitly admitted" peak margin on the call: mid-80s GPM is "a FAIR RETURN," and they "ultimately want to increase visibility and durability of franchise" — i.e. the LTA bargain this page has tracked for four months, stated by management as a trade-off for the first time.** **NEW POSITIVES, and the NBM layer is materially stronger: 5 additional agreements (3 with NEW customers, 2 with existing customers "coming back asking for more") → 8 total datacenter/edge customers; coverage 50%+ of FY27 bits and — new disclosure — 2/3 of FY28 bits (vs Kioxia's ~50% for CY28); duration up to 5 years with a weighted average of 4+ years (new disclosure); RPO $59.9bn at quarter-end → $91.9bn including two post-quarter deals, against buy-side expectations of ~$70bn (TMTB: "$90B getting you to $90 of EPS run rate"); minimum total NBM revenue $93.9bn; financial guarantees stepped up from >$11bn to $16.5bn, mostly on third-party balance sheets, with the guarantee/RPO ratio RISING over time — direct corroboration of Bernstein's part-2 back-end-weighted-collateral thesis; ~80% margins retained even at floor pricing, and management said explicitly "would not assume NBM are a drag on gross margins."** **NEW NEGATIVE, supply-side and net-new: FY27 sellable bit growth guided to MID-TEENS % y/y, BELOW the medium-term mid/high-teens framework, "due to higher inventory levels to prep for NBM's" — so the durability trade costs volume as well as price. - **2026-07-28 (/run-inbox — MS memory webcast, 2026-07-28). 1 intra-quarter row, 1 Current-state block, 1 Risks bullet, 2 Signal cells updated, synthesis extended, 1 Source. No rating, PT or estimate superseded.** **The run adds the sharpest NAND pricing datapoint on this page and, in the same source, the offset that keeps it honest.** **Bull: Joe Moore reports enterprise NAND transacting above 70 cents on multiple deals — up 60-70% from Q2 — after having described the market as "wobbly" only a month earlier; "I'm not sure what's happened, but in the last 30 days that's completely changed."** Two reasons this is more than a price print: the transacted level **runs ahead of the published forecasts already on the page** (BofA/Mohan F4Q26 preview +35% q/q ASP, 07-01; UBS/Gaudois +30% q/q 3Q26 contract, 07-03), and **the LTA pushback and "tough" Q3 negotiations he flagged a month ago have reversed** — which lands directly on this page's central NBM-durability debate, and specifically on MS's own 06-22 flag that some 2028 enterprise-TLC LTA prices were *down* versus 2027. **Bear, from the same webcast and deliberately kept adjacent rather than netted: Sean scopes the strength to the AI segment and flags that the weak segment is the bigger one by bits — "the non-AI part, the traditional tech consumer PC smartphone… that part is much larger… and that's where we're seeing some weakness,"** driven by price ("it is becoming extremely expensive"), not by a demand cycle. Supporting it, and net-new to the page: **TrendForce inventory has ticked up since 2Q, the first time since 4Q last year, and it is at the MODULE MAKERS rather than the producers** — module makers took committed volume they are struggling to move in non-AI NAND. Supply is loosening at the margin too: **industry NAND bit growth high-teens → 20-25% next year, with YMTC adding a third line at 50,000 wafers.** Added as a distinct Risks bullet (channel inventory in the weak segment) rather than merged into the standing "commodity cyclicality" bullet, which is a capacity-normalization argument and a different mechanism. **Positioning flag worth carrying: the analyst delivering the bullish NAND datapoint ranks SNDK LAST of his four — "Nvidia, Broadcom, then Micron, then Sandisk… there's nothing else that's wildly compelling."** Bullish the commodity, fourth on the stock; both recorded. Cross-filed [[MU]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[SKHYNIX]].
- **2026-07-26 (/wiki-ingest — Outlook, weekend batch). 1 intra-quarter row. Nothing superseded.** **The useful item is a same-quarter peer test: Kioxia — SNDK's closest peer and JV partner — reports Friday 7/31, and BofA (Mikio Hirakawa) "remains bullish and expects a valuation re-rate on the heels of expected initiation of shareholder returns and a prospective US listing as well," flagging it a "Best Ideas" name out of Asia.** That matters here for a specific reason: **the "initiate shareholder returns → re-rate" argument is the same capital-returns lever the SNDK bull case rests on**, so Friday is a live read on whether the market pays for it in NAND. BofA's research team described as **"Memory bulls"** into **"a big week for memory — CXMT IPO, Kioxia, Samsung."** ⚠️ Two supply items from the same weekend that cut the other way (filed in full on [[MU]] / [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md)): **Nomura initiated CXMT at Buy forecasting ~18% global DRAM share by end-2028F** — DRAM not NAND, but the same state-supported cost-of-capital dynamic applies to **YMTC, which Susquehanna sizes at 25% of NAND supply by YE28** — and **Apple is lobbying the White House for permission to use Chinese memory in devices sold outside the US.** Tape, two-sided: **SNDK +6% last week** (WDC +9%, MU +8%, STX +8%) with the SOX -19% off its high, but **SNDK is also -37% since the Tech-Momentum ATH on June 22**, among the worst in Jefferies' universe, and memory carries the largest sub-sector derating at **-24% price / -36% P/E.** Diary: **analyst meeting 8/13**, reports **8/5 AMC.**
- **2026-07-24 (/wiki-ingest — 4 sources; additive, no PT/rating/estimate superseded).** **MS · Joseph Moore via MS TMT sales (Wigg, 07-24):** after fresh **data-center purchasing** checks, MS **explicitly defended SNDK (and MU) and called the memory selloff "a compelling entry point"** — **memory is increasingly THE bottleneck to AI builds and agentic-CPU builds, durably so**, with the shortage expected to **intensify in 2027 and again in 2028**. SNDK-relevant: **NAND checks inflecting recently**; **data-center memory prices +25%+ in 3Q** (a deceleration from 2Q's larger increases). Framing to carry: **"LTAs and de-speccing could flatten the amplitude of the cycle vs inflated expectations — but increase duration, which is likely better for stocks in the long run"** (amplitude-for-duration, not pure upside). **The hardest NAND-tightness datapoint of the week — @jukan05 (07-23):** after reporting **YMTC has surpassed Micron and SanDisk in NAND market share, narrowing the gap to Kioxia to just 0.4pp**, and seeing memory stocks sell off, he pushed back — *"NAND is still in short supply, and that shortage will persist. **NVIDIA's CMX racks are currently so constrained by NAND shortages that they cannot even be shipped fully populated.**"* **Both retained: the YMTC share-loss risk is real, and so is the physical enterprise-NAND tightness** — the drawdown was driven by the share headline (and, per Rothschild/Adley 07-24, an unverified Anthropic-ARR read that also dragged the KOSPI -5.7%), not by pricing, which was beating (BofA weekly checks: PC DRAM July contract +15-20% m/m). **Net-new setup markers:** **Mehdi Hosseini "SNDK — Updating Estimates, Thoughts into Earnings/Analyst Day" (07-22)** — the analyst-day pairing matters because that is where the **HBF roadmap** and any long-term model get formalised (cf. the Arete HBF row); **JPM · Joshua Meyers ran an urgent "Storage Earnings BUYSIDE SURVEY: SNDK, STX, WDC" (07-24)** — watch for published results as a read on the bogeys, noting the trio is surveyed together so results read as **relative** positioning; **STX 2Q26 group callback with Erik Woodring on 07.29**; and **SNDK/MU/LITE among presenters at the Nasdaq Virtual Investor Conference in Asia, 08.18-08.20**. Edits: 4 new Intra-quarter Full-log rows (07-22, 07-24 ×3). Cross-filed [[MU]], [[STX]], [[WDC]], [[KIOXIA]].

Cached tokens priced at **10% of compute tokens** across OpenAI/Anthropic/Google. ⚠️ Its own revenue line has **FY29 −11.9%**, contradicting the FY28-30 growth guide it endorses.
